If mapped rows span chapters, the reader opens the mapped anchor chapter with its selected verses. Independent columns open at their chapter. Both Dev builds passed. Live QA remains for noncontiguous and cross-chapter mappings, including Back restoration.

Aligned Compare now shows disjoint selection via selected verse-number controls in both grid and stacked layouts. Clicking a verse number replaces the saved range with that single verse. Transfer to Scripture uses the same selection and checks that loaded rows match the current Compare destination before mapping. Both Dev builds passed. Verify imported disjoint selection appearance, selecting the same anchor again, and switching editions during load in both Dev apps.

Compare entry and column-only edits now retain saved disjoint selections when the underlying passage and anchor are unchanged. In-tab history still records the mode or column change, and passage changes still clear stale selections. Both Dev builds passed. Live QA remains for Reader → Compare → change column order → Back, with disjoint selections throughout.

Compare column headings are now direct passage-picker controls in aligned, stacked and independent layouts, with full-width hit areas and 44-point iPhone minimum height. Column action menus now give iPhone pointer hover feedback. Both Dev builds passed. Verify that selecting a heading edits only the intended column and that long edition names fit at accessibility sizes.

iOS 27 Simulator verified changing a Compare column from its heading. At John 7:51–53, Compare exposed all three verse numbers as selected. After scrolling to the KJVA heading, its picker marked John 7 selected. Choosing John 8 changed only KJVA, with the comparison menu still listing John 7 · LXX and the dock still reporting five tabs. The isolated Dev tab retains this comparison fixture. Mac heading QA and accessibility-size layout remain open.

Aligned Compare keeps column headings actionable when an edition lacks the current passage, including when all columns are empty. It identifies unavailable editions in their heading and omits their repetitive placeholder rows in the stacked layout. Both Dev builds passed. Running-app QA remains for John in LXX and opening the picker from an unavailable heading.

The empty Scripture reader now names the missing passage and edition, offers Choose Passage, and offers an in-place switch when a mapped KJV/LXX counterpart exists. It hides verse actions when no verses have loaded for that destination. Both Dev builds passed. Verify LXX John recovery on both platforms and the Choose Passage path for a text without a counterpart.

Mac and iPhone disable Tag Chapter and text-dependent export actions when a Scripture chapter is unavailable. Copy Chapter Reference remains usable. Compare retains its existing chapter commands. Both Dev builds passed. Live QA remains for unavailable chapter actions and re-enabling after recovery.

iOS 27 Simulator verified unavailable Scripture recovery. From the isolated John 8 KJVA / John 7 LXX Compare fixture, Manage Translations → Open in Reader → John 7 LXX showed Passage Unavailable, Choose Passage and Open in KJVA. Open in KJVA loaded John 7 and kept five tabs. Mac runtime and empty-chapter export menu checks remain open.

iPhone Compare now offers a direct floating Exit Compare control. The first toolbar placement disappeared alongside the navigation controls in a loaded case, so the final control uses a safe-area inset. iOS 27 Simulator verified tapping Exit Compare restored John 7 KJVA in the same tab with the count still five. The action removes compareMode only, preserving column metadata for reentry. Verify reentry, Back and landscape spacing.

iPhone Manage Translations and Exit Compare now share a single top capsule with 44-point controls. This keeps management available when the system toolbar has low space and avoids an extra row for the close button. Mac continues to use its toolbar menu. Both Dev builds passed. Verify capsule spacing and menu presentation in portrait and landscape.

iOS 27 Simulator portrait verified the grouped Compare capsule. Manage Translations opened Add Translation, Change Column Passage, Swap Columns and Open in Reader. After dismissing the menu, Exit Compare returned to John 7 KJVA with five tabs. This run checked control activation through Device Hub accessibility, with landscape and direct touch still open.

Compare now records same-tab column opens and single-column collapse in passage Recents, including mapped selections. These actions and per-verse Open in Reader share the same destination update. Opening one verse explicitly replaces a prior multi-verse selection even when that verse held the same anchor. Both Dev builds passed. Live QA remains for Recents, Back restoration, and opening the same anchor verse from a disjoint selection.

Compare verse actions now include Add Note and Cross References on Mac and iPhone. A note attaches the exact displayed verse and edition. Cross References opens the verse in the same reader tab with its inspector selected. Both Dev builds passed. Live check mapped Jonah destinations, note attachments and Back into Compare.

Compare now exposes Verse Notes and Tag Verse. Verse Notes opens the clicked verse and edition in the current reader with the Notes inspector. Tag Verse opens the shared tag picker directly over Compare and stores legacy-compatible coordinates from the displayed row. Both Dev builds passed. Live QA remains for notes, tagging and the remapped Jonah row.

iOS 27 Simulator verified Compare actions on John 7:2 KJVA. Tag Verse opened the shared picker and Escape returned to the same Compare row without writing a tag. Verse Notes opened the Notes inspector with New Note for John 7:2. Dismissing it showed John 7:2 selected and five tabs still open. Saving tags and notes, cross references and mapped Jonah remain open.

The iPhone Tag Picker now shows its passage as an inline heading and keeps Cancel and Add in a footer outside the list. This addresses the missing title and buttons observed through Device Hub when presented from Compare. Mac retains the existing toolbar. Both Dev builds passed. Verify the target heading and footer with the keyboard up and test tagging an isolated Dev verse.

iOS 27 Simulator verified the updated Tag Picker from the John 7:2 selection bar. The passage heading and both footer controls were exposed. Choosing the existing Dev Chapter Parity test tag enabled Add and saving refreshed its inline marker on verse 2 while retaining selection and five tabs. The test tag remains in the isolated Dev library. Hardware keyboard-up layout and saving from Compare remain to check.

Compare now shows clickable verse-tag chips sourced from each displayed row’s book, chapter and verse. Chips refresh on tag writes and open a reusable Search tab. The lookup follows legacy edition-independent tag coordinates. Both Dev builds passed. Live QA remains for save-to-chip refresh and remapped Jonah rows.

iOS 27 Simulator verified the saved Dev Chapter Parity chip appeared in Compare on John 7:2. Activating it opened the matching tag results with a John 7:2 preview and the dock still showed five tabs, demonstrating Search reuse. Save-to-chip refresh while remaining in Compare and mapped Jonah placement remain open.

Compare now shows an actionable note indicator beside verses with attached notes. It counts notes by the displayed edition, chapter and verse, refreshes after note writes, and opens the verse’s Notes inspector in the same reader tab. Both Dev builds passed. Live QA remains for note creation, indicator refresh and remapped verses.

iOS 27 Simulator verified the Compare note indicator end to end. Add Note from John 7:2 KJVA created a Dev note with that Scripture backlink. Returning to the same Compare tab showed “1 note for John 7:2 KJVA”. Activating the indicator opened the Notes inspector listing that note and New Note for John 7:2. The test note and extra Notes tab remain in the isolated Simulator workspace. Mac and remapped Jonah note indicators remain open.

Stacked Compare now puts the verse-note button and verse menu beside the edition label above each verse. The verse text and tag chips have the full content width, while the grid and independent layouts retain existing control placement. Both Dev builds passed. Live QA remains for large Dynamic Type, note indicators and narrow layouts.

Compare’s SwiftUI scroll views now use the iPhone root dock safe-area inset without a second hard-coded bottom margin. This applies to aligned, stacked and independent comparisons. The continuous TextKit reader keeps its own viewport clearance. Both Dev builds passed. Verify the final verse and controls above the dock in portrait and landscape.

Continuous Scripture no longer subtracts a fixed dock height from its GeometryReader viewport. The root safe-area inset owns dock clearance, and the reader still reserves the selection capsule’s own height. Both Dev builds passed. Recheck the final line and rounded outline above both capsules in the running iPhone reader before treating the spacing as verified.

Supersedes the continuous reader’s dock-inset-only change. iOS 27 Simulator showed that the explicitly sized reader placed verse actions behind the dock even though accessibility still listed them. Restored explicit dock clearance for continuous Scripture. A rebuilt portrait screenshot verified one rounded dashed outline around John 7:51–53, with verse 53 and the outline fully above the separate action capsule and dock. The iPhone Dev build passed. Landscape and Compare scroll-end visual checks remain open.

Landscape QA on iOS 27 reproduced a portrait-width reader surface clipped inside the rotated framebuffer. Relaunch in landscape initially used the full width but remained on the chapter spinner during observation. Replaced the reader’s explicit GeometryReader dimensions with a flexible stack and kept separate padding for the dock and selection controls. Both Dev builds passed. This is a candidate fix: reinstall and verify portrait → landscape → portrait and cold launch in landscape before closing the issue.

### Reader, sidebar, and study feedback

- Both native readers add a faint accent fill inside grouped dashed selections. Mac uses a soft hover tint for inline Scripture links and hover backgrounds for selection buttons.
- Mac sidebar rows show only the LXX edition badge. Tab filtering now includes a type picker. Mac’s KJV/LXX switch exposes a checked state while LXX is active. Scroll anchoring across edition switches is pending.
- Cross-reference groups expand from the full row, Classic and TSKe are direct choices, and Copy keeps the existing reference format. Current cross-reference result rows are KJVA, so LXX preview copying has no current fixture.
- Advanced Search keeps its header at the top and puts options in a scrollable popover. The passage picker suppresses generic classification headings in other writings. Notes opens directly in the rich editor and retains Markdown export and version history.
- Both Dev builds passed. Live UI checks remain for the above changes. Landscape clipping persisted with the flexible frame alone. The iPhone compact-height capsule now uses regular material as a candidate workaround, pending Simulator verification.
- Next implementation areas are per-tab and per-history-entry scroll restoration, cross-space Search/Lexicon Back and Forward, Lexicon controls and occurrence layout, and Notes formatting and richer table/highlight editing. Also apply type filtering to iPhone Tabs and verify both platforms.

iPhone Tabs now filters by tab type independently of space. The filters apply to this workspace and matches from other workspaces. Clear Filters restores both, and unfiltered Recents are hidden while a filter is active. The iPhone Dev build passed. Verify combined filtering, clearing, and cross-workspace matches in Simulator.

Mac verse gutter still shows a pointing hand throughout its clickable area, but the soft hover tint now appears only when hit testing reaches a visible link glyph. Mac Dev build passed. Confirm pointer and tint transitions live with small verse numbers and Strong’s links.

Full Lexicon tabs now show Previous Entry and Next Entry in a floating top-right capsule and omit the duplicate Strong’s number above the lemma. The compact study inspector keeps its entry identifier and inline controls. Both Dev builds passed. Verify navigation, top-bar identity, and the compact inspector live. Filter and occurrence layout, cross-space history, and scroll restoration remain open.

Lexicon Occurrences now groups edition, book, translated-word filters and sort into a single Options menu in the full tab and compact inspector. The current filters are visible below the count and Reset Options clears them together. Result rows have taller hit areas. Both Dev builds passed. Live menu and result checks remain open on both platforms, along with scroll restoration and Search-to-Lexicon Back navigation.

Search Scripture from a Lexicon entry now records the source tab in the Search tab’s Back stack, whether Search creates or reuses its destination. Back returns to the source tab and Forward returns to Search. History skips closed cross-tab targets and preserves existing in-tab snapshots. Both Dev builds passed. Live checks remain for reused Search, compact inspector origin, repeated transitions, and exact scroll restoration.

iOS 27 Simulator verified G5485 Lexicon → Search Scripture → More → Back → G5485 Lexicon → More → Forward → Search: G5485. Back and Forward kept the same seven-tab workspace count. The extra Lexicon fixture remains in the isolated Dev workspace. Mac routing, scroll preservation and compact-inspector history remain open.

Advanced Scripture Search now records the clicked result by tab and query and restores it near the center on return after results load. Both result activation and Open in New Tab add a Back route to the originating Search tab using the Lexicon history mechanism. This is a session-memory result anchor. Arbitrary scroll offsets, per-history-entry view state, persistence across launches and other tab types remain open. Both Dev builds passed. Live QA: scroll deep into G5485, open a result, go Back, confirm row centering, then Forward.

Advanced Scripture Search now stores the active edition chip in the tab state so it accompanies the restored result row. New queries reset it, narrowing scope clears incompatible chips, and external searches protect filtered tabs from reuse. Submitting a passage directly from the Search field also records a Back route. Both Dev builds passed. Verify edition-chip restoration and direct-reference round trips in the running apps.

iOS 27 Simulator verified G5485 Search → LXX chip → Genesis 30:27 → Back restored the LXX chip and brought that exact result back into the visible list. Forward reopened Genesis 30:27 in LXX with the verse selected. The workspace remained at seven tabs. Pixel-exact positioning, deep results, Mac runtime and direct-reference submission remain to verify.

Notes now exposes a Format menu in the Mac and iPhone note toolbar for bold, italic, underline, strikethrough and highlight. It invokes each native editor's selection commands and returns focus to the editor. Both Dev builds passed. Verify live formatting, save/reopen and undo with a synthetic note. iPhone wrapping still needs toggle-off parity, and block formatting, tables and Notes Home redesign remain open.

Mac and iPhone Notes now share UTF-16 inline format edits. Reapplying the same style to the selected text removes its delimiters, and the selection stays on the text. Focused checks covered emoji, asymmetric underline tags, invalid ranges and a trailing caret. Full native check passed with 103 tests and both Dev builds. Live Format menu toggle-off, undo and saving still need verification.

Mac and iPhone Notes now offer Body and Heading 1–3 in Format. The shared editor edit converts the selected paragraphs and preserves Markdown storage. Both Dev builds passed. Live heading, selection, undo and save/reopen checks remain open. Lists and tables remain to implement in the shared Format menu.

Notes Format now includes Bullet List, Numbered List and Checklist on both platforms. The shared edit converts selected lines, keeps untouched following paragraphs and preserves checked items when adding checklist formatting to a mixed selection. Both Dev builds and three focused Swift edit checks passed. Live formatting and undo remain open, along with list continuation, interactive task toggles and table editing.

Resume the active native migration from this handoff, then reconcile its remaining checks with the current branch, STATUS and TASKS before choosing the next change. Keep updating this file after verified work.

Return now continues bulleted, numbered and checklist items in the native note editors. It keeps indentation, advances numbered items, starts a fresh unchecked task, and exits the list on an empty item. Both Dev builds and eight focused Swift edit checks passed. Verify Return and undo in the running Mac editor and with the iPhone software keyboard. Interactive task toggles and table editing remain open.

Native Notes now routes activation of the rendered checklist box to an edit of the existing Markdown marker. Mac uses the native text-link interaction. iPhone checks the rendered checkbox glyph under a tap, even while the editor is editable. Both Dev builds and six focused Swift checks passed, covering UTF-16 offsets, line endings, uppercase checked markers and invalid targets. Verify Mac click and pointer, iPhone tap while typing, undo, save/reopen, and VoiceOver in the running Dev apps. Table editing remains open.

Notes Format → Insert Table now inserts a two-column Markdown table with a header and body row on Mac and iPhone. It preserves existing prose and puts the caret in the first header cell. Both Dev builds and six focused Swift insertion checks passed. Live save/reopen, keyboard movement, undo, grid presentation, and row/column management remain open. The Electron reference uses ProseMirror table controls and GFM pipe table serialization in src/components/notes/pm.

Tab and Shift–Tab move between cells in native Notes tables on Mac and with a hardware keyboard on iPhone. Navigation skips the Markdown alignment row, handles escaped pipes, and preserves the document. The supported form is the outer-pipe format written by both editors. Both Dev builds and six focused Swift checks passed. Verify keyboard dispatch in the running apps. The iPhone software keyboard still needs a way to move between cells, and grid presentation and row/column commands remain open.

Notes Format → Add Row Below inserts an empty body row on Mac and iPhone, using the header's column count and preserving existing cells, alignment and line endings. From a header cell it inserts below the alignment separator. It places the caret in the first new cell. Both Dev builds passed, along with six row-insertion and six navigation regression checks. Verify the command and undo in the running apps. Add/delete column, delete row, and grid presentation remain open.

Notes Format → Add Column After adds an empty column to the right of the selected cell on Mac and iPhone. It preserves existing cell Markdown, alignment, and line endings and moves the caret into the new cell. Short body rows are padded when necessary to keep their contents in the original columns. Both Dev builds and 19 focused checks covering columns, rows and navigation passed. Live menu, undo, save/reopen, row and column deletion, and grid rendering remain open.

Notes Format → Delete Row and Delete Column now work within native pipe tables on Mac and iPhone. Row deletion applies to body rows. Column deletion removes the selected cell and its alignment entry from each applicable row and keeps at least one column. Both Dev builds and 26 focused table checks passed. Live editing, undo, persistence, touch navigation, and grid presentation remain open.

iPhone Notes exposes Previous Cell and Next Cell in the text-edit menu's Table submenu when the requested move has a destination. The action rechecks the text before moving the caret. Mac and iPhone group structural commands under Format → Table. Both Dev builds passed and the iOS 27 Dev app installed and launched on iPhone 18 Pro Simulator. Live menu activation and caret movement remain unverified, along with grid presentation and undo.

Deferred verse reveal now checks that the same passage and exact selection are still active and that an explicit passage jump has not taken over. This closes a race in both TextKit readers when quick range changes leave the previously revealed verse selected. Both Dev builds passed. Live reproduction remains open for rapid selection changes, chapter-end clearance and narrow Mac split panes.

Scripture draws the faint selection fill and dashed accent outline behind verse glyphs on Mac and iPhone. Both platforms share the same opacity values. The existing grouped ranges and scroll clearance are used. Both Dev builds passed. Compare running apps in light and dark mode, especially existing highlights, red-letter text, and adjacent verse selections.

Live Mac Dev verification on 2026-11-14 used the current build and the existing Didache 1 tab. Clicking verse numbers 5 and 6 produced one rounded dashed outline with a faint fill spanning both verses. The complete final line remained visible above the separate floating action capsule. Clicking Clear removed the capsule and left the reader open. This covers a wide light-mode window and sequential clicks. Narrow panes, dark mode, and the current iPhone build remain open.

With the Mac Study Panel open, Didache 1 wrapped more tightly. Selecting verse 6 kept the full final line and dashed outline visible above the floating action capsule. Clear removed the capsule, and the Study Panel was closed after the check. The compact action layout at minimum window width remains open.

Live iOS 27 Dev verification on iPhone 18 Pro used Device Hub accessibility actions in landscape. Starting on Genesis 30:27 LXX, activated verses 42 and 43, then toggled 27 off. The reader showed one rounded dashed outline and faint fill across 42–43, including the complete final line. The verse action capsule and navigation dock were both visible below it. Clear removed the action capsule and left the reader open. Device Hub coordinate input selected a different simulator instead of the verse, so direct touch remains unverified. Portrait and dark mode remain open.

Live iOS 27 portrait verification used the iPhone 18 Pro Dev simulator and Genesis 30:42–43 LXX. Rotation reflowed the two verses under one dashed outline with a faint fill. The entire final line fit above the verse actions and navigation dock. More Verse Actions exposed Copy Reference and Save Verses as Text. Activating Copy Reference returned to the same range, and Clear removed the action capsule. Clipboard contents were not inspected. Coordinate input did not change the selection, so direct touch remains unverified. Dark mode remains open.

The floating verse action buttons and icon-only menus now share control sizing and enabled-state hover feedback. Highlight, Export, and More use the same Mac hover background and pointing cursor as Copy and Clear. iPhone keeps the existing hit targets and pointer hover effect. Both Dev builds passed. Verify the hover visually in the running Mac app and check that nested menu items still use their normal labeled rows.

The verse action button style now applies only to buttons on the floating bar. The compact menu and nested Highlight and Export menus use system menu row styling, so the icon-only bar style no longer leaks into their labeled actions. Both Dev builds passed. Recheck the narrow Mac fallback and iPhone More menu in the running builds.

The Mac Scripture chapter picker and available chapter arrows now advertise a pointing cursor individually. Unavailable chapter arrows use the normal cursor, and the surrounding control group has no pointer override. Both Dev builds passed. Verify cursor changes at the start and end of a book in the running Mac app.

Mac window navigation now gives the sidebar toggle and available Back and Forward actions a pointing cursor in either sidebar layout. Unavailable history actions use the default pointer. Mac Dev build and identity check passed. Live cursor transitions and history round trips remain separate verification items.

Advanced Scripture Search now associates each completed result set with the query, edition scope, match mode, book filter and word-replacement setting that produced it. Changing any of those inputs suppresses stale rows until the matching request finishes. The clicked-result scroll anchor is applied only while the Search tab is active and the matching result is present. Both Dev builds passed. Live checks remain for fast filter changes, deep result → Back → Forward, and preserving arbitrary scroll positions.

Scripture Search now remembers the top visible result by tab and query after scrolling settles. It restores that row on a tab switch and gives a just-opened result priority on the next Back, centering it when there is room. This uses a lazy scroll layout with accessible group headings and restores a row anchor for the current run. Both Dev builds passed. Live checks remain for trackpad and touch scrolling, reopening two differently scrolled searches, and deep Back/Forward. Exact offsets, relaunch persistence, and other tab types remain open.

Live Mac Dev verified Search browsing and history with the existing grace query. Scrolled three pages from the Genesis results into the Ezra/Proverbs results, switched to Didache, and reopened Search in the same result neighborhood. Then opened Proverbs 3:22, used Back to return to Search with that row visible, and used Forward to reopen the passage with verse 22 selected. This verifies row-level restoration and one cross-tab history round trip. Pixel offsets, exact visual centering, two independently scrolled searches, and the current iPhone path remain open.

Scripture Search now keeps independent browsing and clicked-result anchors per tab, query, scope, match mode, book filter, word replacement, testament, edition chip, sort and grouping. A change of context resets the result viewport before restoring that context's own row, if it has one. This is session memory. Both Dev builds passed. Verify switching between two edition chips and returning to each independently scrolled result list on Mac and iPhone.

Notes folder views now remember the top visible note or subfolder after scrolling settles. Positions are independent by tab, folder, sort, grouping, type and status and restore after the library loads. The folder view retains lazy rendering, row menus and accessible section headings. Both Dev builds passed. Live checks remain for returning from a note, switching between folders, deleting a saved anchor, and scrolling a large library. Notes search, the folders overview, Trash, and relaunch persistence remain open.

Live Mac folder testing exposed that opening an already-open note reused its editor tab but the editor's list button returned to that tab's own home. The editor now remembers the source Notes tab for the current run and returns there while it remains available. Live Mac verified All Notes → existing Rich editor test → All Notes selected the original folder tab. Both Dev builds passed. The six-note Dev library did not exercise a deep scroll. iPhone and long-folder row restoration remain open.

In-tab Notes history now distinguishes the folders overview, individual folder locations, and note editors. Mac live verified Notes → All Notes → window Back → Notes → Forward → All Notes. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone Back and Forward and restoration in a long folder remain to verify.

Opening a passage, comparison, note, lexicon entry, or search from the History tab now records History as the Back destination using the same cross-tab route as Search. Both Dev builds passed. Verify each destination and its Forward round trip in the running apps. The parity matrix now reflects verified Search and Notes folder history and distinguishes implemented Notes table controls from their outstanding live checks.

Live Mac Dev verified History → Proverbs 3:22 → Back → History → Forward → Proverbs 3:22 with the verse selected. History → G5485 → Back → History → Forward → G5485 also passed. The initial attempt ran the older process and showed no Back entry, so both checks were repeated after a confirmed relaunch. History row/scroll restoration, comparison, note, search, and iPhone routes remain open.

Returning to History after opening an entry restores the filter text and the opened row when that entry still exists. It also restores the expanded page limit and extends it if newer entries pushed the anchor past the prior limit. The native List stays in place, including iPhone swipe-to-remove. Both Dev builds passed. Verify a filtered History → destination → Back round trip with a deep row and Show Older. Arbitrary History browsing position without opening a row remains open.

Live Mac Dev verified History filtered to G5485 → older G5485 lexicon entry → Back with the filter and matching older row still visible. The quoted G5485 search entry also passed History → Search → Back with the filter restored → Forward to Search. The filtered list fit within one viewport. Deep-row centering, Show Older, and iPhone runtime remain open.

History now keeps filter text and the Show Older page limit per tab for the current run, including when switching tabs without opening an entry. The one-time return row remains separate so Back can still center the opened entry. Both Dev builds passed. Verify switching between two History tabs with different filters and expanded page limits. Arbitrary History scroll offsets and relaunch state remain open.

History now tracks visible entry IDs in the native List and saves the first visible entry when scrolling settles. Returning to the tab restores that row near the top, while Back from an opened entry still gives that row priority and centers it. History filters and pagination remain per tab. Both Dev builds passed. Verify row visibility and scroll-phase callbacks on Mac and iPhone, including wheel scrolling, Show Older, and returning after the saved row is deleted. This is session row memory without pixel offsets.

Opening a Lexicon occurrence now records the origin tab for Back and Forward. In a full Lexicon tab it also remembers the opened occurrence and expands the initial page far enough to reveal it when returning. The compact inspector uses the existing tab route when the passage opens in another tab. Both Dev builds passed. Verify a deep occurrence round trip in both apps. Occurrence filter and arbitrary scroll restoration remain open.

Full Lexicon occurrence return now restores the edition, book, translated-word filter, sort, and expanded page limit before scrolling to the opened row. User-driven option changes still reset pagination. If the word replacer changed while away, the old translated-word filter is dropped. Both Dev builds passed. Live filtered and deep occurrence round trips on Mac and iPhone remain open, as does preserving arbitrary Lexicon browsing position.

Full Lexicon now keeps occurrence options and page limits by tab and Strong’s entry during the current run, including ordinary tab switches. The opened-occurrence anchor remains a one-time Back destination. Both Dev builds passed. Live filter and paging persistence, arbitrary scroll restoration, and relaunch behavior remain open.

Full Lexicon now captures the vertical scroll offset when user scrolling ends and restores it for the same tab and entry after the results load. The opened-occurrence return row takes precedence. Filter and sort changes invalidate the previous offset because they change the layout. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac wheel/trackpad and iPhone scroll restoration, particularly near the end of a paginated entry, remain open. The compact inspector and relaunch persistence remain open.

The Mac Dev scroll test found that phase callbacks missed the tested scroll input and returned G5485 to the top. Lexicon now captures offsets from scroll geometry once the entry has finished loading and restoration has been requested. The revised Mac Dev test scrolled two pages, switched to Search, and returned to G5485 at the same reported scrollbar fraction (0.7831858407079646). Both Dev builds passed. iPhone, expanded pagination, and filtered Back remain open.

Lexicon reload now remembers whether word replacement changed independently of the saved options, which can be updated before the scroll task runs. Back can expose the opened occurrence even after the translated-word filter is cleared. With Most Matches enabled, it makes all loaded rows available to locate the entry in the new order. An ordinary tab return discards an offset from the old rendering. Both Dev builds passed. Live word-replacement and deep filtered Back checks remain open.

Lexicon return pagination now uses the same filtered and sorted sequence as the displayed occurrences. Back expands only through the page that contains the opened row, including after word-replacement changes. Both Dev builds passed. The settings-change round trip and exact centering of a deep row remain to verify live.

Live Mac Dev verification expanded G5485 beyond its first page and opened Ephesians 2:7 near the end of the displayed occurrences. Back restored G5485 with Ephesians 2:7 visible and the scrollbar at the bottom. Forward reopened Ephesians 2 with verse 7 selected. This covers pagination and the cross-tab round trip. The viewport was clamped at the end, so exact mid-list centering, iPhone behavior, and the word-replacement change remain open.

Notes Home now restores the search query, match mode, scope, type, and status from session memory for each tab. Returning from a note to its list can rerun the same search. Both Dev builds passed. Live editor return and separate search state in two Notes tabs remain to verify. Search result scroll and persistence across launches remain open.

Notes search now records the opened result by tab, folder, search options and sort order. Returning to the list restores the result near the center after the matching search loads, provided the note still matches. Both Dev builds passed. Verify a round trip with a result below the first viewport on Mac and iPhone. Browsing offsets without opening a result remain open.

Notes search now saves the vertical offset from scroll geometry by tab and search configuration during the current run. A clicked result still takes priority on return. Search uses the folder view's lazy styling and retains note context menus. Both Dev builds passed. Verify deep Notes search scrolling and the editor return on Mac and iPhone. Relaunch persistence remains open.

Opening a note from a Notes list now records a cross-tab Back route when the app reuses an existing editor tab or opens a separate editor tab. Same-tab opens continue to use location history. This lets window Back and Forward use the same source list that the editor’s All Notes button uses. Both Dev builds passed. Verify both routes from a filtered Notes search on Mac and iPhone, including a pinned list that opens an editor separately.

Live Mac Dev verified that searching All Notes for “Grace” and opening the existing Rich editor test reused its editor tab. Window Back restored the search text and the matching row, and Forward reopened the same editor. The fixture results fit in one viewport. Deep-result centering, pinned-list routing, and the iPhone round trip remain open.

The native Scripture readers now save the vertical offset when a chapter view is removed and restore it when the same tab and chapter are reconstructed. Explicit verse jumps retain priority. This is session memory for the single-chapter TextKit readers and stores offsets separately by edition. Both Dev builds passed. Verify switching away from an unselected long chapter and returning on Mac and iPhone. Edition remapping, Compare, selected-verse return behavior and relaunch persistence remain open.

The LXX toggle now attempts to carry the top visible verse into the counterpart edition without selecting it. Same-edition tab returns retain their saved offset. The switch uses matching verse numbers where available and the existing Jonah mapping when the mapped verse belongs to the destination chapter. A missing anchor falls back to the chapter start. Both Dev builds passed. Live toggle and flip-back checks remain open, particularly combined Psalms and chapter boundaries where verse-number alignment is approximate.

2026-11-14 reader follow-up from the user

- iPhone Scripture now shows an edition badge in the passage capsule only for LXX. The accessibility label still names the edition. Tapping a rendered verse glyph toggles the verse, while inline links and active text selections retain their own handling. Verify direct touch, rapid adjacent taps, long-press word selection, and Strong’s links in the iPhone Dev app.
- The shared passage picker now opens the sole book’s chapter grid when a one-book text is selected. Verify Apocalypse of Elijah on both platforms, including changing the edition from inside the compact picker.
- The Mac LXX toggle reuses the already confirmed source edition during reciprocal switches. Verify the toolbar during repeated flips and when switching a chapter whose reverse mapping differs.

### Design decisions made this session (record in DECISIONS.md if you disagree)

1. **Global Mac cursor hook** — `apple/BereanMac/ClickableCursor.swift` swizzles `resetCursorRects` on `NSButton`, `NSSegmentedControl`
   and AppKit's private `NSToolbarItemViewer` (looked up with `NSClassFromString`; skipped if missing). Toolbar items are SwiftUI
   `ToolbarItemHostingView`s inside the viewer, so per-view `pointerStyle` cannot reach them. Viewers containing an editable text
   field/view are skipped. **Update 19:55:** disabled toolbar items now keep the arrow — the hook reads the viewer's `item` (`NSToolbarItem.isEnabled`,
   guarded by `responds(to:)`), and `ToolbarCursorWatch` re-checks after each window update and invalidates a viewer's cursor rects
   when its enabled state flips (verified: Back arrow → hand after navigating). A DEBUG-only, env-gated dump (`BEREAN_CURSOR_DEBUG=1`) writes the window view tree to
   the sandbox tmp (`~/Library/Containers/com.berean.native.mac.dev/Data/tmp/berean-views.txt`) — useful for any future AppKit
   hosting question.
2. **`clickableCursor()`** modifier (in `ScriptureView.swift` next to `InteractivePlainButtonStyle`) for SwiftUI menus/pickers that keep
   their native look; plain buttons keep `.buttonStyle(.interactivePlain)`. Don't apply it to containers that hold text fields.
3. **Lexicon search query is not persisted** (`AppModel.lexiconSearches`, in memory) to avoid writing a new tab-state key that the
   Electron app doesn't know. A Lexicon tab with `strongsNum: ""` shows the search view (the original app accepts an empty entry too).
4. **Daily notes' title is read-only** in the editor and shows the long date. Renaming a daily note would detach it from its day.
5. **Psalm superscription data is generated** (`Packages/BereanKit/Sources/BereanBible/PsalmTitleData.swift`) from
   `src/lib/psalmTitles.ts` with a small Node script (`node --experimental-strip-types` importing the TS module and printing Swift
   dictionaries). Regenerate the same way if the TS changes; do not hand-edit.
6. **Annotation hiding** writes the existing tab-state key `hiddenAnnotations` (string array, same as Electron). `kjva_italics` is not
   implemented (token-level in the original).

### Gotchas learned

- Synthetic right-click menus: open with `cliclick rc:x,y`, drive with arrow keys + Return (clicking menu items synthetically is
  unreliable); press Escape (`key code 53`) when done or AppKit tracking can hang the next command.
- `screencapture` images are 2× (Retina): divide pixel coordinates by 2 (or by the displayed scale) and add the window origin
  {100,60} to get `cliclick` points.
- `⌘[` only works when the content (not the sidebar) has focus: click into the content first.

The override resets when the passage or selection changes. Full native check passed (113 Swift tests and both Dev builds). Live Mac Dev verified Exodus 20:10, 12 → chapter notes → selected verse notes, with both verses still selected and New Note switching its displayed attachment each time. iPhone inspector interaction and note sorting parity remain open.

The shared Notes inspector has a compact Notes Options menu for Current Passage / All Notes and By Verse / Date Edited / Date Created. It keeps the creation target on the current passage. All Notes shows up to the 500 most recently edited notes, matching the legacy panel cap, and labels the limit when more exist. The sort preference is local. Both Dev builds passed. Mac Dev verified that All Notes surfaced a note from another chapter while Exodus 20:10, 12 remained selected, that Date Created moved an older-edited note ahead of a recently edited note, and that Current Passage returned to the original selection. The Mac Dev sort was left on Date Created. iPhone live interaction and legacy panel search remain open.

The study Notes inspector now filters the active scope by case-insensitive title and body text. A clear button and a no-results state are available without changing selection, scope, or sort. The All Notes search covers the same recent 500 notes as the legacy panel. Both Dev builds passed. Mac Dev verified that searching for “exod 20:11” found the synthetic note by its body, a nonmatching query showed No Results, and Clear restored the list while Exodus 20:10, 12 remained selected. iPhone keyboard and narrow inspector interactions remain to verify.

### 2026-11-15 Codex continuation

Integrated Claude's `696920c6` checkpoint before publication. Its Lexicon Find implementation is the baseline. Codex added a visible Find button on both platforms, headword and pronunciation matching, consistent matching against expanded BDB notes, and previous/next matching-section navigation. Both Dev builds passed on the integrated branch. Mac Dev verified Command-F focus and H1286 “Berith” advancing from 1/2 to 2/2 sections. The check used the bundled corpus in a Dev process without the library lock. iPhone interaction, occurrence/related-term matching, and visual highlight contrast remain open. The superseded local implementation is preserved on local branch `codex/lexicon-find-local`.

Lexicon Find now includes derived-term rows and currently loaded occurrence rows, respecting edition, book, and translated-word filters. Matches in these previews expand while Find is active, and the arrows jump to each matching row without changing the occurrence list. Both Dev builds passed. Mac Dev H1286 “Berith” advanced from 1/3 to 3/3 and reached Judges 9:46 on the third result. A derived-term-only query, iPhone interaction, and matching rows outside the currently loaded page remain to verify. Use Show More or Show All to include additional occurrence rows.

Lexicon Find now checks results beyond the first occurrence page within the current filters. When a match needs a later page, it renders through that page and then scrolls to the row. It searches the occurrences returned by the service, which still has its existing cap, and does not change filters. Both Dev builds passed. On Mac Dev, H1285 initially showed Show More without Malachi 3:1. Finding that passage surfaced one match and loaded its row without pressing Show More. iPhone interaction and navigation with active occurrence filters remain to verify.

Compare columns now offer Move Earlier and Move Later on both platforms. Mac additionally has a drag handle on each column header that accepts drops only within that comparison. Reordering writes the existing column records in the new order, preserving pinned passages and metadata. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac Dev verified Genesis 1: the first column had Move Earlier disabled, and Move Later placed LXX before KJVA. Coordinate dragging was unavailable in the current remote UI session (`noWindowsAvailable`), so the drag gesture remains unverified. Also check iPhone controls, saved reorder after relaunch, and three-column layouts. Drag resize is still open.

Mac side-by-side Compare now has resizable column dividers in aligned and independent layouts. The aligned grid uses the same width for each header and its verse cells. Divider movement transfers width between the two neighbors, and reordering carries their in-memory widths along. Both Dev builds passed. Mac Dev aligned Genesis 1 verified the accessibility adjustment path: 409/409 → 433/385 → 409/409 points, with both verse 1 actions still present. Mouse drag, independent layout and resize-after-reorder still need live verification. Widths are view-local, matching the legacy desktop comparison.

Compare supports an optional Sync Scrolling toggle when different-passage columns have separate side-by-side scroll views. It uses the legacy `compareSyncScroll` tab-state key (missing means enabled). The pane receiving direct input drives the others by top visible verse number, with nearest earlier verse or first verse as fallback. The aligned grid and stacked layout already share a scroll view. Both Dev builds passed. Live scrolling remains unverified: Mac Compare opened, but the UI session reported intervening input before the independent-column test. Retest by stepping only the LXX column to Genesis 2, scrolling KJVA Genesis 1, and checking the LXX top verse with Sync on and off in Manage Translations. Verify the same in iPhone landscape. Portrait independent columns still render as stacked sections, and within-verse synchronization is not implemented.

A two-column iPhone comparison can now use independent portrait panes at 320 points and up with standard Dynamic Type. The compact panes place verse controls above the text and show short edition labels over passage titles. At accessibility text sizes or when additional columns don't fit, Compare uses the stacked layout. Where panes fit, turning Sync Scrolling off now separates same-passage columns too. Turning it on keeps the verse-aligned grid for same-passage comparisons and mirrors separate panes for different passages. Both Dev builds passed. Mac Dev opened the aligned view, but the UI bridge timed out opening Manage Translations. The toggle transition, portrait layout, and scroll coupling still need live testing.

Searching “created” went from 246 results to 3 after applying the tag. Clear retained the tag and showed the Genesis 1 chapter row. Opening the row and pressing Back restored tagged Search with the filter intact. Multi-tag Any/All, long-list anchor placement, and iPhone interaction remain open.

Mac scratch-library QA now covers two-tag matching and tagged verse previews. “QA Creation” marks Genesis 1, and “QA First Verse” marks Genesis 1:1. The same selected tags showed the chapter with Any and only Genesis 1:1 with All. The verse row showed its KJV + Apocrypha edition and the opening sentence. Opening that row and pressing Back restored the All filter and verse preview. This one-row fixture verifies the return destination and filter, while long-list scroll placement, edition changes, and iPhone UI remain open.

The native Mac and iPhone Scripture reader now supports the legacy `kjva_italics` annotation preference. Scripture Display can hide the supplied words using parsed KJVA tokens, preserving Strong’s data on the remaining words and leaving untagged passages intact. Verse copy and selected-verse export use the same setting. Toggling KJVA supplied words no longer requests a fresh chapter. Both Dev builds and the targeted Swift regression passed. Live Mac scratch QA toggled the setting off and on for Genesis 1:2, removing and restoring italic “was” in “darkness was upon.” Live iPhone, clipboard, partial highlight remapping, whole-chapter export, and Compare annotation parity remain to check.

Compare now honors edition-specific annotation visibility in aligned and independent verse rows, and Copy Verse applies the same filter. Scripture Display resolves the actual Compare columns, including the implicit KJVA/LXX pair, and shows each edition's controls. Compare retains original verse text for character-highlight offset mapping. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA toggled the KJVA supplied word off in Genesis 1:2, then relaunched the final build and verified both KJVA and LXX controls appear in the default comparison. Live LXX filtering, Compare copy output, iPhone interaction, and highlight mapping with hidden tokens remain open.

Compare now runs the same Psalm superscription projection as the reader before constructing aligned or independent rows. The KJVA title is separated from the tagged verse body, and LXX title-only leading rows fold into the first body verse while keeping its actual verse number. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA opened Psalm 3, verified the KJVA and Brenton superscriptions and the LXX first-body verse action at 3:2, then switched Sync Scrolling off and verified both titles and LXX 3:2 remained in the independent panes. iPhone layout, title copy behavior and independent missing-LXX markers remain open.

Independent Compare now marks gaps in an LXX passage before the next present verse, using the same eligibility and annotation helper as the standalone reader. The aligned grid retains its empty counterpart cell for absent verses. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA opened Exodus 32 in Compare, confirmed the aligned KJVA 32:9 cell, and turned Sync Scrolling off. The independent LXX pane showed “— v.9 not in LXX —” above the actions for Exodus 32:10. iPhone layout and a comparison with unrelated pinned passages remain to verify.

Copy Chapter, Save Chapter as Text, and Share Chapter now carry the originating tab’s annotation settings into the shared chapter formatter. The reader, Compare, and export reuse the same projection for bracket-style annotations. KJVA export removes supplied words through the token formatter. Chapter exports keep their existing verse numbering and edition labels. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA copied Genesis 1 with supplied words hidden and pasted it into a scratch note, verifying verse 2 read “darkness upon” and retained its verse number. Save, Share, LXX bracket filtering in exported text, and iPhone interactions remain to verify.

The reader now keeps its loaded chapter rows for highlight coordinate mapping and applies bracket annotation visibility at display and copy time. Toggling annotation visibility does not re-fetch the chapter or reapply its initial verse selection. Character ranges map against the original verse, and older word-index highlights first resolve their bounds in that original text before mapping to the shown words. The same highlighted-text helper serves Compare. Both Dev builds passed. Live partial-highlight QA remains: mark text after a supplied span, toggle the span off and on, and verify the highlight stays on the same words. Perform the same check in Compare, with a legacy word-index highlight and an LXX Psalm title projection.

The full native Swift suite passed all 121 tests, including the text-offset conformance fixtures.

Numbered live workspaces now lead with a tab preview in the Mac switcher, iPhone Workspaces list, and matching Mac command palette rows. The alias is derived at display time from the existing preview order; the stored Workspace or Session number still appears in the details and remains searchable and editable. Custom names and snapshots retain their titles. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA created Workspace 2, opened Proverbs 8, and verified the switcher listed “Proverbs 8 (KJVA)” while the rename action still said “Workspace 2.” iPhone layout and the behavior of multiple numbered workspaces with identical leading previews remain to verify.

The Mac workspace switcher now offers Find Workspace or Tab, with separate matching workspace and open-tab sections. Return opens an exact stored workspace name first, then prefers a matching tab for other queries. Identical leading previews in the compact switcher include their stored workspace names on the primary line. Mac Dev built and live scratch QA verified two Proverbs 8 workspaces appeared as Workspace 2 and Workspace 3, and searching Workspace 2 showed both its workspace and tab results. Return switched from Workspace 3 to Workspace 2. Saved-snapshot search and large-workspace performance remain to verify.

iPhone Workspaces search now puts workspace matches before Open Tabs, hides New Workspace while searching, and shows an empty-results state. Keyboard Search opens an exact stored workspace name first, then a matching tab or workspace. The iPhone Dev build passed and the new build installed and launched on the iOS 27 iPhone 18 Pro simulator. Live touches, submitted-query navigation, and the long-list layout remain unverified because the Simulator window is unavailable to the host UI bridge.

Inactive-tab cleanup now starts with Keep Recently Used Tabs on. It excludes up to five recently used open tabs in the current workspace, in addition to the existing pinned and per-Space active protections. Turning it off lets the user include those tabs. Turning it back on removes protected tabs from the current selection, and the archive operation revalidates eligibility before writing. Exact-duplicate review still keeps a matching copy open. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA visited Psalm 3, switched to Exodus 32, and confirmed Psalm 3 was protected by default, became selectable when protection was off, and was removed from the selection when protection was restored. No tabs were archived in the live check. Large workspace, duplicate-mode combinations, and iPhone interaction remain open.

Recent-tab cleanup now keeps its history per workspace in local preferences. Activity in other workspaces can no longer evict the cleanup history of a workspace that has recorded its own visits. It falls back to still-open tabs from the prior global history for existing installs. Closing or moving tabs excludes them from the source workspace’s protected candidates, and removing a workspace removes its local history. Both Dev builds passed. Live verification of global-history eviction remains open: visit five tabs in one scratch workspace, switch away and visit more than twenty tabs elsewhere, then return and check protected candidates.

Exact-duplicate cleanup now prefers a protected recent copy over an older inactive copy, after active and pinned copies. The row explains when the matching recent copy stays open. Changing recent protection in duplicate mode clears the pending selection because it can change which copy is eligible. The archive operation uses the same priority when it revalidates. Both Dev builds passed. Live reproduction remains open: in a scratch workspace duplicate a tab, visit enough other tabs that only the later copy remains recent, and check that the older first-in-sidebar copy becomes the cleanup candidate.

Mac scratch QA verified duplicate cleanup with recent protection on the current build. In Workspace 4, duplicate Romans 8, then open John 1 through John 5 as distinct tabs. Exact Duplicates Only offered one Romans 8 row with “Recently used copy stays open.” Selecting it enabled Archive 1, and turning protection off cleared that selection. Re-enabling protection, selecting the row and archiving left one Romans 8 open and exposed Archived Tabs. Restore placement and iPhone interaction remain to check.

Mac's tabbed Scripture inspector now supports two resizable vertical panes. Each toolbar menu moves Notes, Lexicon, or Cross Refs between panes or closes the second pane. It reads and writes the existing `rightPanelSlotBTabs` and `rightPanelSlotB` fields and accepts the legacy one-key format. Strong’s opens and navigation route to the pane owning Lexicon, and moving Lexicon between panes moves its open entry. The iPhone inspector remains a single sheet. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA opened Notes in John 5, moved Cross Refs to a second pane, verified both were present, and closed the second pane to restore the three-tool selector. Live QA remains for Strong’s routing and transfer, resize, relaunch, keyboard navigation and older persisted slot data. Dragging between pane headers and independent legacy note cursor/filter state remain open.

Live Mac scratch QA on the split inspector: opened John 5 with Notes in the main pane and moved Lexicon to the second pane. A Strong’s click loaded G32 (ἄγγελος) in pane two while Notes stayed visible. Moving Lexicon back restored the single inspector with its entry controls still present. Moving it out again and relaunching restored Notes above pane two, with G32 and its definition visible. This verifies current native save/restore and Lexicon transfer. Legacy-state fixture restore, divider resizing, drag-and-drop, and iPhone interaction remain open.

Mac study pane headers now provide draggable tool buttons and highlighted drop targets when both panes are open. Drops validate that the source belongs to the same inspector and that the move leaves a tool in the main pane. The existing menus can create the second pane and move tools without a pointer gesture. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA verified the new headers restored Notes and Lexicon with G32 and that Move Lexicon to Main Pane still merged the inspector. Coordinate drag attempts did not change assignments, so pointer drag remains unverified. Check both directions with a physical pointer, plus divider resizing and drag-out creation parity.

The inspector slot decoder now lives in BereanWorkspace and preserves the saved order of tools in the second pane. Compatibility fixtures cover the older single-key split through TabFields persistence, duplicate and unknown tools, a stale active tool, protecting the last tool in the main pane, and merging back to one pane. Pointer drag, divider behavior, and restoring an original-app workspace end to end remain open.

Both Dev builds and all 123 Swift tests passed for the inspector persistence change.

iPhone Tabs now has Select in both grid and list. Select visible inactive tabs and use the floating Archive button to file them as one recoverable group. The active and pinned tabs are protected, selection is pruned when filters change, and individual close/swipe actions and other-workspace results are hidden during selection. List Edit remains available for Custom Order. The iPhone Dev build passed, installed and launched on the iOS 27 simulator. The host Simulator window is still unavailable for live input. Verify selecting in grid/list, switching layouts, narrowing filters, toolbar fit at 320 points, and restoring the resulting archive group. Bulk permanent close remains open.

iPhone bulk archive now shows the number archived with an Undo action directly in Tabs. Undo uses the archived group’s original workspace and saved tab order. The archive operation returns the actual group created, so the receipt reflects eligibility at write time. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA separately verified the shared restore path: selecting the archived Romans 8 group restored its position before John 1 and brought the workspace back to eight tabs with both copies present. The iPhone receipt, Undo tap, and narrow-width layout still need live checks.

Mac and iPhone Dev now register `berean-native-dev://` for external links. The scheme is separate from Production and Capacitor/Electron Dev. External and editor links share the existing parser and destination handler for passages, notes, Strong’s entries, search, daily notes, and workspaces. Both Dev builds passed and their built Info.plists contained the dedicated scheme. Parser tests passed, including the new scheme. Live macOS URL dispatch opened Romans 8:1–3 and selected the range in the scratch app. iOS 27 Simulator accepted the search URL through `simctl openurl`, but its destination was not visually verified because the Simulator window was unavailable. Spotlight, App Intents, widgets, Share Extension, and unsupported viewer routes remain open in I24.

The reader can now copy Native Dev links from Chapter Export, selected-verse export, and Compare verse actions. Links include the edition and the original chapter/verse numbering. A disjoint selection copies one link per contiguous range on separate lines, so skipped verses stay skipped. The URL builder and parser round-trip the range, edition, and supported prologue chapters. Existing Markdown note links retain the legacy scheme.

Both Dev builds and all four deep-link tests passed. Mac scratch QA copied Romans 8:1–3 from the selected-verse menu into a scratch note and verified the exact URL, including KJVA. Chapter, Compare, disjoint-selection clipboard output and iPhone interaction remain open.

Pasted passage URLs now resolve in Mac Command-K, iPhone Go, passage selection, and other shared reference fields. External dispatch and paste share edition and range validation. Several copied verse URLs in the same chapter and edition combine into one selection without including skipped verses. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA pasted separate Romans 8:1 and 8:3 links into Command-K, saw one Romans 8:1, 3 result, and verified Return selected exactly those verses. iPhone paste and mixed-chapter clipboard cases remain to check. Mixed-chapter or mixed-edition input deliberately does not produce a combined passage.

The iPhone reader now supports optional pull-to-change-chapter navigation. Drag past the top or bottom and release after the glass cue changes to “Release to open.” It reuses the existing chapter-step history and traversal, respects the haptics and Scripture Display settings, and stays inactive while verses or text are selected. Both Dev builds passed and the iOS 27 simulator installed and launched the build. Physical drag threshold, cancellation, short chapters and book boundaries remain unverified because the Simulator window is unavailable to the host UI bridge. Continuous chapter scrolling and Mac pull remain open.

Mac now has the same optional Pull to Change Chapters setting as iPhone. The AppKit reader only arms from a direct, precise trackpad gesture at a chapter edge and commits after release. Momentum, unphased wheel events, and active Scripture/text selection are excluded. Both platforms use the same glass cue and chapter-step history. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA toggled the setting off/on and verified the existing Next Chapter button advanced Romans 8 to 9. Physical trackpad pull, cancel and momentum behavior, iPhone gesture checks, and the separate continuous-reading mode remain open.

Backward chapter pulls now request a one-time landing at the end of the previous chapter, matching the legacy paged reader. Mac and iPhone consume the request after the destination loads and calculate its bottom after TextKit lays out the text. Arrow buttons, horizontal swipe navigation, and ordinary history restores keep their existing placement. Both Dev builds passed. Physical pull and landing checks are still open on both platforms, especially at the start of a book, on short chapters, and when pulling back into a previously visited chapter.

The chapter-pull cue now names the actual destination, including the next or previous book at a boundary. The reader preloads both adjacent destinations from installed chapter numbers. Its buttons and pull actions use the same lookup, and a pull does not arm before a destination is known. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA verified Next from John 21 opens Acts 1, and Previous returns to John 21. The named cue and physical pull remain unverified. Continuous scrolling still needs a separate multi-chapter reader path.

Mac floating search now uses the clear Liquid Glass variant, with regular glass under Reduce Transparency and the existing material fallback on earlier macOS. The panel has a soft shadow to separate the translucent edge from the reader. The Mac Dev build passed. In the light appearance over John 21, scratch QA verified visible blur through the panel, immediate typing after Command-K, and outside-click dismissal. Dark appearance and the Reduce Transparency appearance still need visual checks. The content backdrop dims, while the native window toolbar still needs separate treatment.

The Mac command palette now dims the native toolbar background alongside the reader and disables the underlying app toolbar controls while open. Regular Liquid Glass gives the panel a stronger blur after live QA showed verse lettering bleeding through Clear Glass. Focus is requested as the palette appears. The current Mac Dev build passed and was tested with only one scratch-library instance running. Command-K followed immediately by typing “Romans 8:1” captured the full query, the toolbar dimmed, and clicking outside restored the toolbar over the same chapter. Dark appearance and Reduce Transparency still need visual checks.

Mac and iPhone Notes now offer Format → Table → Delete Table, matching the legacy editor's whole-table command for outer-pipe Markdown tables. It removes the active block in one editor edit, keeps adjacent paragraphs and separate tables intact, and leaves the caret at the removed block's start. Both final Dev builds passed. Seven focused Swift checks covered UTF-16 offsets, escaped pipes, CRLF, end-of-document deletion, a neighboring table, malformed markup and fenced examples. Live menu activation, undo and save/reopen still need checks on both platforms. Native grid rendering remains open.

Live Mac scratch QA for `cec84643` verified Format → Table → Delete Table from a body cell. The table disappeared and both surrounding paragraphs remained. Command-Z restored the header, separator, and body exactly. Command-Shift-Z removed them again, and switching to Scripture and reopening the note retained the edit. iPhone live verification and grid rendering remain open.

iPhone Notes now exposes Previous Cell and Next Cell directly above the software keyboard while editing a table. The buttons take the Quote and Link slots, disable at the respective table edge, and use the same destination and scrolling as hardware Tab and the text-edit menu. Quote and Link remain available from the note toolbar. The iOS Dev build passed and installed and launched on the iOS 27 iPhone 18 Pro Simulator. The UI bridge could not bind the Simulator app, so verify the accessory flip from prose to a table, the two edge states, caret movement, and return to prose on a visible simulator or phone.

Mac floating search shows separate ⌘ and K keycaps in place of the Actions caret. The first ⌘K opens search, and a second opens result actions when the selected row supports them. Open and Actions now use 14-point labels, 20-point keycaps and a taller shared glass capsule. Live Mac scratch QA verified immediate typing, opening the action popover with a second ⌘K, opening Romans 8:1 with Return, and the larger footer in the final build. Popover action activation, keyboard focus restoration and narrow window fit still need targeted verification.

The Mac sidebar's material now extends through the title bar. The Scripture toolbar groups display/edition, study toggles and chapter actions, with Tag Chapter in the same menu as copy, export and share. The passage picker remains in the top bar. Note detail separates navigation, editing and note management. Lexicon separates entry navigation from lookup and utility actions. Live Mac scratch checked the sidebar through the title bar, Scripture groups and availability of Tag, Copy and Share inside Chapter Actions. Verse capsule controls share monochrome styling and menu icon sizing, but live light-mode review still showed the native Highlight and Export triggers slightly lighter than direct buttons. Use the same styled button trigger for a popover if native menu chrome cannot be normalized without losing menu behavior.

Toolbar source review covered the root navigation and sidebar, Scripture, Compare, Lexicon, Notes home/detail, History, Go, Tags, Archive and tag editing. Advanced Scripture Search and Calendar use in-content controls. Next visual pass should check Mac overflow at minimum width, Notes home creation versus selection, Compare's translation control alongside Scripture groups, Lexicon navigation versus lookup, and iPhone sheet dismissal versus confirmation. The Mac screenshots covered Scripture and note detail in light mode. Remaining surfaces, dark appearance, VoiceOver, and iPhone interaction need live checks.

Native table headers now use visually distinct weight and fill, with toned-down structural dividers and shared cell parsing that preserves escaped pipes. Existing Markdown and edit offsets remain intact. Both Dev builds passed and all seven MarkdownLiveSpans tests passed. This is a styling improvement to the live editor. Aligned editable grid rendering and live table styling QA remain open.

Mac floating search now dims the title bar with one lighter tint. The content scrim stops at the title bar instead of stacking with its tint. The footer is 58 points tall, the glass buttons have additional vertical padding, and the ⌘/K and Return keycaps are 23 points with larger glyphs. Labels remain 14 points. Mac Dev built and live scratch QA verified the lighter title bar, new footer, and immediate typing over Romans 8.

Mac verse Highlight and Export now use the same button styling as Copy and Tag, with anchored popovers for their actions. The existing iPhone menus remain in place. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA verified matching icons, both popovers, and that the Copy Native Dev Link action closes Export and preserves the verse selection. Escape from Highlight cleared the verse selection during the check. Verify whether Escape should dismiss only the popover, plus Highlight writes, Save/Share presentation and the compact layout.

Mac Scripture now keeps track of which verse popover is open and temporarily removes the Clear Selection shortcut while it is presented. Escape first dismisses Highlight or Export and preserves the selected verses. A subsequent Escape clears the selection. The Mac Dev build passed. Live scratch QA verified both popovers with an active Romans 8 selection and verified the second Escape clears the range after Highlight closes. The compact layout and Save/Share presentation remain open.

Live Mac scratch QA at `c1261266` exercised the verse Export popover with Romans 8:1–2. Save Verses as Text opened the native panel with the expected filename. After choosing the scratch directory, export wrote the reference and exactly verses 1 and 2. Share Verses opened the system share picker. No share destination was selected. The full action bar was accessible with the Study Panel open. The UI bridge did not resize the window at its edge, so the compact overflow path remains unverified. The reopened reader also showed the first selected line above the viewport while verse 2 remained visible. Check explicit range jumps and restored scroll positions separately before changing top-edge behavior.

Mac single-chapter Scripture scrolls behind the transparent title bar and its floating glass controls. The reader reserves windowed toolbar clearance at the chapter start, using the measured overlap when available and a 52-point minimum in the windowed reader. Both search dimming regions use the same tint strength, and the Layout menu has no caret. AppKit native buttons and toolbar items replace their original cursor rectangles with one pointing-hand rectangle over the visible control. Live Mac scratch QA saw text behind the toolbar while scrolling and verified Layout still opens. Cursor flicker during a continuous hover sweep, the dimming match in dark mode, and Compare title-bar underlap remain open.

The grouped verse outline animates at about three points per second on Mac and iPhone. The faint fill stays still. The timer runs only during active reading with a selection and respects Reduce Motion. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch captures of a stationary John 6:1–2 selection changed across frames and showed a single dashed boundary. iPhone animation and the system Reduce Motion toggle still need live checks.

The native scroll target now includes the range end so a new jump with the same opening verse can re-evaluate which lines fit. Mac uses the first actual line fragment as the requested start. At the top of John 6, both opening verses were fully clear of the toolbar after scrolling to the top, but the initial explicit John 6:1–2 jump still placed the top of the selection above the viewport. Investigate the ordering of the asynchronous jump, TextKit layout, and the same-passage refresh before claiming range-jump placement.

Mac range jumping now converts line and verse bounds from the TextKit document view to the enclosing clip view before positioning the scroll. A scratch trace found AppKit can place the document origin at a negative offset after applying the title-bar inset. The fix uses the actual document top for chapter starts, converts the same-passage reading anchor and selection reveal calculations, and stores a document-relative scroll offset. Mac Dev built and live scratch QA verified John 9:1–2 below the toolbar, John 9:14–18 fully framed in the reader, and John 9:39–41 entirely above the verse action capsule. Returning to saved positions after a tab switch or edition flip, resizes, and physical chapter pulls still need follow-up QA.

Tab returns now restore the cached Scripture scroll even when a verse range is still selected. The reader saves the selection with its scroll position and compares it when rebuilding the tab. Explicit verse opens still jump, including reopening the exact range already active through Command-K. Mac edition flips sample the visible reading area below the toolbar and align the corresponding verse below the glass in the next edition. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA restored Exodus 34:1–2 after switching to Notes and back at identical scroll-bar position (0.6315), explicitly reopened it near the chapter start (0.0046), then flipped KJVA → LXX → KJVA while staying in the same area (0.6315 → 0.6068 → 0.5894). The LXX toggle tracked both editions. Exact pixel restoration across edition flips, other history routes, and iPhone interaction remain open.

An immediate KJV–LXX round trip now restores the saved offset for the original edition on Mac and iPhone. The reader records the offset where it lands in the other edition. If the offset changed before flipping back, the app maps the newly visible verse instead. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA in Exodus 34 verified that the KJVA scroll bar returned to exactly 0.6514195583596214 after visiting LXX at 0.6067647058823528. A second test scrolled to the top in LXX and flipped back, correctly landing at the top in KJVA. Live iPhone, divergent versification, resize and history combinations remain open.

The iPhone single-chapter Scripture toolbar now offers a glass KJV/LXX flip control beside the passage capsule when the counterpart chapter is installed. It follows the reader chrome as it tucks away, shows the LXX selection visually and through accessibility, and uses the same edition change and scroll restoration as Mac. Both Dev builds passed and the iOS Dev app installed and launched on iPhone 18 Pro Simulator (iOS 27). Live tapping, small-screen fit, VoiceOver and the iPhone scroll round trip remain open. Cross-chapter verse mapping is still an open gap. The current toggle resolves a chapter from ChapterMap, so Jonah 1:17 KJV ↔ Jonah 2:1 LXX still needs the live reading anchor to participate before deciding the destination chapter.

KJV/LXX flip now captures the mounted reader position before changing editions. It uses the existing exact Jonah verse correspondence to choose the destination chapter, preferring a single selected Jonah verse when its first line is visible. An immediate flip back can return to the exact original chapter and offset even if the chapter numbers differ. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA followed Jonah 1:17 KJVA to Jonah 2 LXX and back to the end of Jonah 1. A separate Exodus 35 check confirmed exact same-chapter scroll restoration (0.8326612903225806 both ways). iPhone live verification, toolbar stability during cross-chapter loading, and other books with partial-chapter mappings remain open.

The KJV/LXX reader flip now uses verse correspondences for the merged and split Psalms, Joel 2:28–32, the end of Malachi, and Jonah. The mapping follows the bundled Brenton verse order, including the Psalm 9 heading and the final three reordered verses of Malachi. Both Dev builds and the focused bidirectional Swift regression passed. Live Mac scratch QA verified Psalm 116:10 → LXX Psalm 115 → KJVA Psalm 116 and Malachi 4:4 → the end of LXX Malachi 3 → KJVA Malachi 4. Other verse-level differences still use existing chapter mapping. Live iPhone interaction and toolbar stability during cross-chapter loads remain open.

The Scripture toolbar now loads installed KJVA and LXX chapter numbers once for the current book and derives the counterpart synchronously. The toggle no longer waits for a fresh chapter query while loading a different chapter of that book, including split-chapter flips. On iPhone the existing More → Switch edition action now routes through the same live reader capture as the toolbar. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA verified successive Psalm 116:10 → LXX Psalm 115 → KJVA Psalm 116 flips with the toggle available, and confirmed Didache omits it. Continuous frame capture and live iPhone interactions remain open. The Command-K query “lxx: Psalm 151” opened text search, so the Psalm 151 no-counterpart case still needs a passage-picker check.

Native passage fields now recognize installed edition IDs followed by a colon, plus the Septuagint and Brenton aliases. Trailing LXX is also supported. Conflicting edition qualifiers do not produce a passage match. The parser allows Psalm 151 only when explicitly qualified as LXX. Both Dev builds and five reference tests passed, including the golden suite. Live Mac scratch QA opened `lxx: Psalm 151` as Scripture and confirmed no counterpart toggle. It also selected the second verse with `Psalm 151:2 LXX` and opened `kjva: Romans 8:1`. Live iPhone Go and source-scoped keyword search remain open.

Edition-qualified keyword queries now scope Scripture search from Mac Command-K, iPhone Go and the advanced Scripture Search field. The query field shows the words, the scope control shows the edition, and search history retains the typed qualifier for replay. Explicit passage navigation still takes precedence when the input resolves to a book or passage. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA searched `lxx: creation` from Command-K and saw eight Brenton-only results, then submitted `kjva: everlasting covenant` from the Search field and saw 24 KJVA-only results. Live iPhone, history replay and the legacy space-only source aliases remain open.

Submitting words from advanced Scripture Search now adds a History entry as well as a recent-search suggestion.
