For a single-edition scope the stored query includes the edition qualifier, so opening it from History restores the same words and edition. This uses existing history fields. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA submitted “voice of joy” within an LXX-scoped tab, found `lxx: voice of joy` in History, reopened it with Brenton as the only scope, and verified Command-[ returned to History. Live iPhone replay and restoring multi-edition scopes, book filters, or other advanced options from the activity log remain open.

History now records the book and edition for whole-book Find and for advanced Scripture Search submissions filtered to one book and one edition. Replay uses the existing history book field and edition-qualified query. It reuses a matching open search or recreates it after close. Both final Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA searched for “waves and storm” in LXX Psalms, confirmed History recorded the Psalms scope, replayed into the existing search, then closed it and replayed again. The recreated search showed the Septuagint scope, active book filter and two results. Live iPhone replay, direct Find invocation, multi-book scopes and other advanced options remain open.

iPhone Advanced Scripture Search now opens Options as a dedicated scrollable sheet with a Done button. It dismisses the main search keyboard before presenting and keeps the controls available when the book filter brings up its own keyboard. Mac retains its scrollable popover. Both Dev builds passed, and the iOS 27 Simulator installed and launched the updated app. Live sheet opening, narrow portrait fit, large Dynamic Type, and book entry while the keyboard is up remain to verify.

The iPhone Search Options sheet now presents long option choices in menus, where they have room at larger text sizes. Mac retains segmented controls for short choice sets and uses the menu for Testament. Reset Options also restores Scripture order, grouping by text, and verse-only context. The context reset updates the existing app-wide Search context preference. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA changed all three result settings, confirmed Reset appeared, and verified all three defaults after resetting. Live iPhone large-text fit, menu accessibility, and scrolling with the book field focused remain open.

Archive Inactive Tabs now offers an eligible-count tab type filter on Mac and iPhone. Select Matches works with the type and text filters together. After an archive the sheet displays the actual archived count and can Undo to the source workspace. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA selected and archived Genesis 1:1, verified the receipt and clicked Undo to restore it. With recent protection temporarily off, the type menu counted three Scripture and three Search candidates, and selecting Scripture showed only the three Scripture tabs. The UI bridge exposed the Undo action as a list row, so the successful check clicked the visible button. Live iPhone, VoiceOver and larger workspace checks remain open.

Archive Inactive Tabs now shows how many selected tabs are hidden by the current filters and offers Deselect Hidden Tabs. The Archive button still counts the entire eligible selection, and users can build a selection across multiple filters. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA selected Genesis 1:1 and Psalm 3, filtered to Psalm, saw “2 selected · 1 hidden by filters,” and used Deselect Hidden Tabs. This left Psalm 3 selected and changed the button to Archive 1. No tabs were archived in this check. The Mac accessibility bridge still represents some inline actions as list rows, so coordinate activation was used. Live iPhone and VoiceOver remain open.

The inactive-tab cleanup sheet now moves a selection into another live workspace on Mac and iPhone. It rechecks the same inactive, pinned, recent and duplicate rules as Archive. The move keeps tab IDs, state and display order, and preserves the receiving workspace's active tab. Single-tab context-menu moves use the same model path. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA moved two Compare tabs from Study to Workspace 2, verified their order and Compare state beside the still-active Proverbs 8 tab, and quit and relaunched to verify persistence. Live iPhone, large batches, and single-tab moves from the current active row remain open.

iPhone Tabs selection now includes a workspace move menu directly in the floating selection bar. It acts on the visible, eligible selection and uses the shared inactive-tab move, preserving active and pinned tabs. A dismissible receipt and accessibility announcement state how many moved and where. An earlier archive remains undoable after dismissing the move receipt. iPhone Dev built, installed and launched on the iOS 27 iPhone 18 Pro Simulator. Live grid and list selection, compact-width layout, move receipt, VoiceOver and destination checks remain open because the Simulator UI is unavailable to the bridge.

iPhone Tabs selection now offers Select All, Select Matches and Deselect All for the eligible tabs in the current view. It applies to grid and list and keeps active and pinned tabs protected. The sheet now computes its filtered tabs once for each render and skips cross-workspace searches during selection. The iPhone Dev build passed, installed and launched on the iOS 27 Simulator. Live filtered selection, menu layout, and VoiceOver still need checks.

Mac Command-K and iPhone Go now list matching archived tabs below the normal destinations. Results match the tab and its archive group, including the source workspace name when known. Selecting a result restores the original tab through the existing archive path. Explicit New Tab mode keeps its usual results. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA archived Job 7:1–2, found the Archived Tabs result separately from Go to, then restored the tab and confirmed verses 1–2 were still selected. Live iPhone Go, multi-tab group recovery and archive matches across different workspaces remain open.

Archived search results now offer Restore to [original workspace] when that live workspace is available. Mac exposes it through the result context menu and the Command-K Actions popover. iPhone Go exposes it through the long-press menu. The standard Restore Tab action still restores into the current workspace. Both actions reuse the group restore logic and retain the other archived records. If the ID is already open, single-tab restore selects the existing tab and clears the stale entry from that group. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA archived Job 8:1–2 in Workspace 2, switched to Study, selected the archived result through Command-K Actions, and verified the restored passage reopened in Workspace 2. Live iPhone, multi-tab groups and duplicate-ID recovery still need verification.

The slowly moving selection outline and the caret-free Layout menu are already on this branch from `97294e9d`. The outline moves at three points per second on both apps and observes Reduce Motion. Live Mac scratch QA reopened the Layout menu and confirmed its choices remained available. The Mac floating-search dimming now overlays the native title bar at the same strength as the content scrim. This keeps the existing transparent reader toolbar and dims the native glass above it. Mac Dev built and the scratch app showed an even light-mode backdrop behind Command-K. Dark mode, fullscreen, and live iPhone outline motion remain to verify. PR #22 still targets main, #23 targets `native/m7-export`, and draft #24 targets `native/m6-navigation`.

Mac ⌘L opens floating search with the current location selected. Scripture prefill uses the passage, Lexicon uses Strong’s or its live query, Search uses the stored query, and an open note uses its title. A compatible current tab takes priority over an already open match. Other destination types use their existing spaces and can return through Back. Pinned entries and specialized reader/search configurations retain their protection. The remaining visible “Notes Home” command label is now “Notes”; existing route and preference identifiers remain valid. Mac scratch QA verified first-press selection of Job 9, typing Job 10 in the same tab with the count unchanged, Back to Job 9, opening H7225, preselecting H7225, then Back to Job 9. Command-K still starts with an empty focused field and offers Notes. Compare, pinned tab, and search/note initial selection still need dedicated QA.

Notes Format → Highlight offers Default plus six colors on Mac and iPhone. Named highlights use the legacy Markdown mark class and both editors render the additional colors found in older notes. The formatting helper recolors a fully selected highlight, safely skips partial or multiline overlaps, and keeps the default shortcut and iPhone keyboard and context actions. Seventeen standalone Swift checks passed for selection and markup handling. Both Dev builds passed and Mac scratch QA confirmed that Format → Highlight → Blue writes the correct legacy markup. iPhone interaction, Mac live recoloring, and long multisurface editing sessions remain open.

Mac and iPhone Settings → Notes now expose Detect Scripture References, Check Spelling, and Default Status for New Notes. The editor applies the toggles while a note is open. Detection controls automatically generated links, while explicit note links retain their normal styling and routing. The default-status picker uses the existing note-creation setting, so existing note statuses and note identities do not change. Unknown nonempty legacy status values remain visible until explicitly replaced. The native check passed (128 Swift tests, four XCTest cases, both Dev builds). In Mac scratch QA all three controls responded, and a read-only check confirmed the two toggle values and that the synthetic new note inherited In Progress. Live link-click behavior, spelling marks, iPhone controls, and other I19 settings remain open.

The optional Notes Trash confirmation is available on Mac and iPhone. It covers the editor, list context menus, iPhone list swipes and bulk actions. It follows the legacy default (off) and prompts only when at least one selected note has body content. Cancel preserves the note and any selection. Both Dev builds and all 132 tests passed. Live Mac scratch QA enabled the setting, opened the editor confirmation for a synthetic note, and verified Cancel kept its text visible. Live bulk, iPhone, and affirmative/restore interactions remain open.

Reading settings now offer Copy Verses When Highlighting on Mac and iPhone. It defaults off. With the setting enabled, applying a verse color in Scripture or Compare copies the displayed passage using the corresponding existing Copy action. Multi-verse selections copy together with the reference. Applying a partial highlight copies the containing verse. Removing highlights leaves the clipboard alone. Both Dev builds and all 132 tests passed. Mac scratch QA enabled the setting, applied Blue to Romans 8:1–2, and pasted the reference and both displayed verses into floating search, then dismissed it. Live Compare, iPhone, and partial-selection clipboard checks remain open.

Settings → Notes now offers an editor body font on Mac and iPhone. It uses the existing notesFontFamily preference and the native reader’s font mappings. Both note editors restyle open content when it changes. Headings follow the chosen family and code/table spans keep their monospaced styling. Saved web-only families remain selectable as the current value and use the existing native fallback. Both Dev builds and all 132 tests passed. Live Mac scratch QA changed System to Monospace with a note open and confirmed the body visibly switched while retaining its exact text. Live iPhone, other font families, mixed formatting, and UI-wide font parity remain open.

Native Mac and iPhone Notes now use the existing note icon field via Note Details → Icon, with study presets, custom single emoji, and Remove Icon. The editor heading and Notes list/peek show the icon. The Notes list context menu now offers Print and calls the same system print flow as the editor. The first Mac test revealed the native context menu could prevent sheet presentation; deferring presentation until after dismissal fixed it. The native check passed on both platforms and the final Mac build passed. Mac scratch QA applied Dove, verified the icon rendered in the list and was stored with the original note body intact, then opened and canceled the system print sheet from the Notes row. Live custom input, icon removal, iPhone interactions and multi-page printing remain open.

Fixed Mac Lexicon layouts now use the same selected-verses and dictionary-entry view as the inspector. An entry can return to the exact selected verse list, and changing the selection reopens the list. Pending verse fetches are canceled when the selection changes. Both Dev builds and all 132 tests passed. Live Mac scratch QA chose Scripture · Lexicon over Cross Refs, opened G3772 from Genesis 2:1 LXX, then used Selected Verses to return to the same verse without changing its selection. Other fixed presets and the shared iPhone pane still need live checks.

Inline Strong’s links in Scripture now have Open, Open in New Tab, Copy Strong’s Number and Copy Reference (Strong's G123) menus on Mac and iPhone. New Tab records a Back path to the source. On Mac, opening Strong’s from a layout with no Lexicon switches to the standard study panel; layouts that already show Lexicon retain their layout. Both Dev builds and all 132 tests passed. Mac scratch QA activated G2316 from Genesis 2 LXX in Reading and confirmed the standard inspector showed the entry with the reader selection intact. The UI bridge exposed the inline links but did not capture their AppKit right-click menu, so menu actions, iPhone long-press and occurrence-row parity still need live checks.

Lexicon occurrence rows now offer Open Verse, Open in New Tab, Copy Verse and Copy Reference on Mac and iPhone. Both open actions use the existing edition-aware passage and return-to-occurrence path. Copy Verse includes the reference and full displayed text; LXX retains its reference qualifier. Related Strong’s rows also have Copy Reference. Both Dev builds and all 132 tests passed. Live Mac scratch QA used H1286 → Judges 9:46, verified the full clipboard text, opened a new tab (six to seven), and used Back to return to H1286 with the occurrence visible. Live iPhone, compact inspector, LXX clipboard, and restoring a row below the first result page remain open.

Plain Scripture searches only reuse tabs with matching edition scope and books and no extra verse tags, testament, word mode, or focused edition. Book-scoped History replay uses the same compatibility check, so a saved plain search does not silently acquire filters from another tab. Both Dev builds and all 132 tests passed. Live Mac scratch QA searched “joy and gladness” (52 results), added QA Creation (0 results), and ran the same plain query from Command-K. It opened separately with 52 results and returning to the original tab kept QA Creation and zero results. Live book-scoped replay with a conflicting candidate and iPhone remain open. The History row still restores only the saved query and its supported single-edition/single-book scope. Persisting arbitrary filters needs a backward-compatible history payload.

History now replays the advanced Scripture Search settings captured when a query is submitted: edition scope, books, match mode, testament, verse tags and their match rule, sort and grouping. It reuses an exact open search or makes a new tab. Native options live in bounded device-local preferences keyed by History entry ID and isolated by library. The legacy history query and database schema are unchanged. Native delete/clear remove the corresponding preferences, and submissions with different filters can be distinct even when their legacy query is identical. Both Dev builds and all 133 tests passed, including the deduplication regression. Live Mac scratch QA submitted “joy and gladness” with QA Creation, closed the search, and replayed the newer History row to recover the tag and zero results. Back returned to History and the older row still opened the plain query with 52 results. App relaunch, several simultaneous filters and live iPhone remain open. Result context stays a device-wide display preference; History does not store it.

iPhone root content now registers a native leading-screen-edge Back gesture when tab history has a prior destination and the keyboard is down. It uses the same Back traversal and haptics preference as the dock, supports either layout direction, and requires a predominantly horizontal inward swipe. A tab without history stays open. The iPhone Dev build passed and launched on the iOS 27 simulator. Device Hub tapped Genesis 6:8 from Search, but automation drags did not move it back and also did not scroll the reader in the vertical control attempt. End-to-end recognition remains unverified. Next check: use a direct iPhone swipe from Search → result, then test inner chapter swipes, selection, keyboard and modal behavior before marking I7 done.

The full Lexicon tab’s info button now uses the same native toolbar button style as Find, Search, and Copy. The compact inspector keeps its fixed-size control. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA visually checked the enlarged control alongside its neighbors and opened the guide on H1286. Also verified direct iPhone Simulator taps on the bodies of Genesis 6:9 and 6:10 extended the existing 6:8 selection to one continuous outlined range with the action capsule visible. The iPhone guide appearance remains to check live.

iPhone successful chapter changes now give one selection haptic through the existing preference, including arrow, margin-tap and horizontal chapter-swipe routes. Pull-to-turn keeps the readiness tick it already has. Successful KJV/LXX flips and Compare column steps also use the preference. The async Compare step checks the active tab and the column destination again before applying. Both Dev builds passed. Device Hub verified direct iPhone Simulator taps advanced Genesis 6 → 7 and then switched to Genesis 7 LXX while the LXX indicator appeared. Physical haptic feedback, Compare step, and gesture checks remain open.

Compare now looks up installed chapter numbers by edition and book once while those columns are open. The per-column Previous and Next commands use that list synchronously, including chapter gaps, and disable at either end of the book. Both Dev builds passed. In Mac scratch QA, Genesis 1 LXX disabled Previous, Next moved only the LXX column to Genesis 2, and Previous returned it to Genesis 1 while KJVA stayed on Genesis 1. Live iPhone, final-chapter boundaries, and editions with nonconsecutive chapters remain to verify.

Compare now remembers the reading position per tab and comparison during the app session. Shared-scroll layouts restore their vertical offset, and separate columns restore their top verses. Mac scratch QA scrolled the Genesis 1 comparison so verse 6 was near the top, switched to Lexicon and back, then opened Genesis 1:7 from the Compare row and used Command-[. The same viewport returned in both cases. Both Dev builds passed. Separate-column, iPhone, resize, and mode-change restoration still need live checks. This cache is in memory, like the existing Lexicon reading position cache.

Compare now carries reading positions through column edits. A pure reorder retains the shared scroll offset. Unchanged independent columns carry their verse positions through reindexing, and an old column binding cannot write into a new comparison. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA scrolled Genesis 1 until verse 10 was near the top, then used Swap Columns and saw the LXX/KJVA order reverse with verse 10 at the same vertical position. Independent column moves and live iPhone remain to verify.

The Mac workspace capsule and iPhone Tabs workspace row now show the existing passage-based label for numbered workspaces. Rename starts with that label as an editable suggestion. The numbered name remains visible in workspace details and Mac help until the user saves a rename. Current-workspace lookup now reads only the live session instead of parsing saved snapshots. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA checked that Workspace 2 showed Genesis 1:1, that Rename prefills it, and that Cancel preserves Workspace 2. Live iPhone and narrow title-bar checks remain open.

Workspace search recognizes the displayed passage labels of numbered workspaces. Return prefers a uniquely matching stored name or displayed label. Direct tab matches precede matches that also use workspace terms, and searching a generated passage label alone does not list unrelated tabs. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA searched Genesis 1:1 and got Workspace 2 plus the matching Compare tab only. Entering the full displayed Genesis 1:1 (KJVA) label and pressing Return opened Workspace 2 with its Compare tab still selected. Live iPhone and duplicate display-name checks remain open.

Bulk archive restore now resolves records whose tab ID is already live. It leaves the live tab in its current workspace and restores only the other tabs. A group containing only already-open tabs no longer stays stuck in the archive after Restore. The plan preserves future-format records that this build cannot decode. Both Dev builds, the 133-test suite, and the updated focused restore regression passed. The fixture covers mixed existing/new records and restoring a single existing record; live duplicate-group and iPhone interaction still need checking.

Archive browse and Go search now index the passage-based labels of source workspaces as well as their stored names. The existing local archive-placement record captures the label at archive time, so searches can still find a group after a label changes. Display labels remain concise and restore still uses the source workspace ID. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA archived H1286, found it by both Genesis 1:1 (KJVA) and Workspace 2, and restored the selected Lexicon tab, returning the workspace to ten tabs. Live iPhone and search after an actual source rename remain open.

Mac Command-K and iPhone Go now rank archived exact titles first, followed by direct tab matches and then group/workspace matches. The limit is applied after ranking. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA archived Genesis 1:1 before H1286, whose newer archive matched only by its workspace label. Searching Genesis 1:1 listed the exact archived passage first. Both archives were restored and the temporary passage tab was closed. Live iPhone ranking remains open.

iPhone Go now offers an exact open passage in another live workspace, with the workspace name shown below the tab. Mac Command-K uses the same lookup. Both prefer the active Scripture tab if there are multiple exact matches in a workspace, and the iPhone New Tab flow keeps its existing behavior. Both Dev builds passed and the simulator app launched. Next live check: open the same edition and passage from a different workspace, select the match, and confirm it switches to the existing tab with the same tab count.

The full Lexicon tab now uses a larger standalone information glyph inside its native toolbar group, beside Find, Search and Copy. Both Dev builds passed. The current Mac scratch build exposed the updated toolbar button and opened the guide from H1286. The updated iPhone toolbar still needs a visual check.

Shared floating glass controls now use an opaque, system-colored surface with a thin outline when Reduce Transparency is enabled. This covers the verse actions, iPhone passage controls and dock, and other views that use the shared capsule. Tucked iPhone passage controls and dock also leave the accessibility tree while hidden. Both Dev builds passed and the iPhone 18 Pro Simulator launched the updated build. To verify live, select verses over red-letter Scripture, enable Reduce Transparency on the simulator or test device, and check the capsule in light and dark appearance. With VoiceOver enabled, scroll until the dock tucks away and verify it leaves and subsequently rejoins the focus order. Keep IOS-UI-030 and SHARED-UI-020 open pending those checks.

Selecting a verse on Mac or iPhone now leaves the viewport in place if that verse is taller than the reading area above the floating controls. Shorter verses still scroll clear of the action bar when needed. This applies to tap or click selection. Opening an explicit passage still uses its own scroll path. Both Dev builds and identity checks passed. Live test pending: use large Scripture text in a short Mac window and on iPhone in landscape. Select a long verse near its first line and confirm it stays in view, then select a shorter verse near the bottom and confirm it moves above the capsule.

Strong’s Scripture search now uses the rendered KJVA word positions when matched tagged tokens are available. This fixes an edge case where tagged rendering and plain replacement produce the same snippet after dropping a word, but the original occurrence positions would highlight the wrong words. A conformance case uses a phrase rule and a Strong’s rule that both render Psalm 23:1 as “Yehovah is my shepherd.” and asserts that the snippet highlights Yehovah and shepherd. The occurrence-index fallback is still used when no rendered match is available. For MAC-UI-008, visually check highlighted result snippets on both Dev apps with Word Replacer enabled and disabled, including a combined Strong’s and plain-word query.

Verification for the Strong’s snippet change: `npm run native:check` passed 130 Swift Testing tests, 4 XCTest cases, and both Dev builds with identity checks.

Calendar now offers a seven-day view on both native apps. Entering it shows the current sunrise-aware week; the arrows move by civil weeks, including across months and years, and Current Week returns to today. Switching back restores the previously browsed month. Week navigation stays local to the view and uses the existing daily-note date keys. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA browsed December, switched to Nov 15–21, advanced to Nov 29–Dec 5, and switched back to December. Live iPhone, date-to-note activation from week view, and large-text layout remain to check. N12’s device location acquisition and continuous daily scroll are still open.

The native Calendar’s month heading now opens a month/year picker, following the original Calendar widget. It updates the existing month key and leaves the selected note alone. Both Dev builds and identity checks passed. Live Mac scratch QA verified localized month names, rejected year zero, changed December 2026 to February 2024 in one jump, and found February 29 in the resulting grid. Check compact iPhone presentation and keyboard dismissal next.

Calendar now includes Browse Daily Notes on Mac and iPhone. The timeline shows Markdown previews by day, adds earlier and later dates in weeks, and opens or creates a note only when chosen. Back returns to the Calendar timeline at the opened date, using an in-memory anchor and the existing daily-note date keys. After a File Provider read stall, a fresh local checkout passed all 134 tests and both Dev builds. Mac scratch QA opened February 1, 2024 from the timeline, used Back to return to the same date, and loaded another week through February 10. The iPhone 18 Pro Simulator launched the build. Live iPhone navigation, nonempty Markdown preview, and automatic edge loading remain to check. The original checkout recovered and is the source for the commit.

Daily Notes timeline now loads dates when the paging control itself comes into view, so the earlier edge works even when the top visible day is still several rows away. The paging controls are still available for explicit activation. Note action labels include the full date, Today's heading is announced, and open/create has a 44-point target. Both Dev builds passed in the separate local checkout while an older Xcode build held the original build database. Mac scratch QA inspected “Open Daily Note for Thursday, February 1, 2024” and scrolled both ways without pressing a paging button. New rows extended through January 11 and February 17. Live iPhone layout, VoiceOver speech, and nonempty Markdown preview remain open.

Daily Notes Markdown previews now route Berean links inside the native app. Scripture, note, Lexicon and search destinations can return to the timeline through Back, and the source day is saved before following the link. Other web links retain the system open action. Both Dev builds passed. In Mac scratch QA, a February 1 note rendered a Markdown heading and a Genesis 1:2 LXX link. Clicking it selected verse 2 in the LXX, and Back returned to the February 1 preview. Live iPhone, note/Lexicon/search links, and external links still need coverage.

Version History now adapts to compact iPhone widths with a native version picker, a full-width Markdown preview, and a visible Save Version Now action. Mac and regular-width iOS use the split layout. Changing versions starts each preview at the top, and manual save errors use the existing error presentation. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator live QA opened Version History from a scratch daily note, verified the empty state and unclipped controls, saved a manual version, and opened the restore confirmation. Saved content preview, switching between multiple versions, actual restore, and regular-width presentation still need live checks.

Note version restoration now commits the pre-restore snapshot, note update, and pruning atomically, and only accepts versions tied to the live note being restored. It emits change notifications after commit. The version sheet reports stale notes or versions and routes database failures through the app’s error handler. The new SQLite regression rejects a foreign version, forces an update failure to verify snapshot rollback, then retries and verifies the recoverable prior text. The full native check passed 135 tests and both Dev builds. Live restore of a populated version and subsequent recovery through the Before Restore entry remain to check on iPhone.

iPhone 18 Pro Simulator verified populated Version History and recovery on the build containing the atomic restore change. In an isolated Dev daily note, saved “First simulator draft” manually, changed the current text to “Second simulator draft,” restored the first version, and confirmed the editor showed the first draft. Reopened Version History, confirmed the Before Restore preview showed the second draft, then restored it and confirmed the editor showed the second draft. This closes the live compact iPhone restore check. Regular-width presentation and broader editor undo/redo QA remain open.

Note printing now renders the same Markdown blocks as native previews for both apps, including headings, emphasis, code, links as labeled text, and tables. Mac continues to use the system print panel and its PDF menu. iPhone uses the system markup print formatter. Both Dev builds passed, and Mac was rebuilt after aligning short notes at the top of the printed page. Live Mac scratch QA opened Print in the native Share menu and saw a dated title, formatted heading and link label in the system preview. Cancel returned to the note. Live iPhone preview, long-note pagination, and table rendering still need checks.

iPhone 18 Pro Simulator live QA opened the formatted note print flow through the editor’s overflow → Share → Print. The system AirPrint Options sheet showed the human-readable daily-note title, scratch text, and Page 1 of 1. Closed without printing. A subsequent long Markdown fixture appeared in the editor but did not survive a simulator process restart, so it did not establish table rendering or pagination. To isolate, save a manual version of a populated long scratch note, reopen it and verify its contents, then inspect Print. The restart observation does not establish a reproducible autosave defect.

The note editor now flushes pending text and title changes on scene backgrounding in addition to its ordinary debounce and navigation saves. Both Dev builds passed. On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, edited the isolated scratch note, sent the app to Home, terminated the backgrounded process and relaunched. The edit persisted. Repeated with a longer Markdown fixture and reopened it, then verified the system print preview showed two pages. The input path may have smart-converted the table separator, so true table rendering remains unverified. Background-and-relaunch does not exercise an abrupt foreground process kill.

The shared note editor now preserves typed Markdown table separators, including alignment colons, when Auto Em Dash is enabled. Prose conversion still works, including in table cells containing prose. The full native check passed 136 tests and both Dev builds. On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, typed an aligned two-column table, backgrounded and relaunched the Dev app, saved a version, and verified its Markdown preview showed a rendered Item / Value table with Test row / 42. The system print preview also displayed the table; no print job was sent. Live Mac typing and detailed printed-table pagination remain open.

Native Markdown previews and note printing now render the editor’s plain and colored highlights, including legacy palette colors. The same inline Markdown parse retains emphasis and links around highlights, and inline code stays literal. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA visually checked a bold yellow highlight, a legacy sky-blue highlight, and literal == delimiters in code in Version History. Both colors were visible in the Mac system print preview, which was canceled. Live iPhone highlight appearance and detailed mixed/nested formatting remain to check.

Native note previews and print now recognize checklist states, numeric list markers and nested indentation. Checked and unchecked items have distinct markers and accessibility labels, while the existing editor remains the place to toggle them. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA verified a checked item, a nested unchecked item and an item numbered 3 in Version History and the system print preview. No print job was sent. Live iPhone rendering and wrapped items at larger text sizes still need checks.

Native Markdown previews and print now group the legacy Note, Tip, Warning, Important and Caution callout syntax, retaining optional labels and rendering the body as Markdown. Screen previews use distinct symbols and colors, and printed callouts show the type and title. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA verified a titled Tip with a checked task, an untitled Warning, and the following independent paragraph in Version History and Print Preview. No print job was sent. Live iPhone, callout insertion controls, and nested imported callouts remain to check.

Notes now offers Insert → Callout on Mac and iPhone with Note, Tip, Warning, Important and Caution. The action wraps the current paragraph or selected lines in the same Markdown syntax used by the original editor, keeps neighboring paragraphs separate, and uses the native editor’s normal edit path. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA chose Important with the caret in the final paragraph and verified the existing text remained inside the resulting callout while the previous Warning stayed separate. Live iPhone insertion, selection across multiple paragraphs, and Undo remain to check.

Return now continues block quotes and callouts in both native editors. An empty quoted line exits one level, leaving a real blank boundary at the top level, and checklist or numbered items inside a quote continue within it. Both Dev builds and eight focused Swift edit checks passed. Live Mac scratch QA pressed Return in an Important callout, added a second quoted line, then pressed Return on the empty quote and typed an independent paragraph. Live iPhone keyboard paths and nested-quote editing remain to check.

Study Trail now allows editing stop notes and personal connection notes on Mac and iPhone. It writes the legacy cached_subnote and user_note fields, scopes writes to the visible trail, and preserves the inferred connection reason. The migrated SQLite fixture verified both edits, rejected cross-trail writes, and preserved the reason. All 136 tests and both Dev builds passed after correcting the save closure. Live Mac scratch QA created a trail, recorded Genesis 2, and saved a stop note from its pencil action. User-note editing on an existing connection and live iPhone remain to check. Map, automatic recording, and connection relationship edits remain open.

Study Trail now offers Connect to a Stop for the active Scripture passage, Lexicon entry, or note. It adds a top-level legacy connection, preserves Scripture verse ranges including previously saved range ends, checks trail membership, and skips exact duplicates. The legacy connection schema has no destination edition field, so Scripture uses the source stop’s edition where available. Both Dev builds and all 136 tests passed. Mac scratch QA added the active H1286 entry to the Genesis 2 stop and saw its connection row. Live iPhone and reopening a saved Scripture range remain to check. Automatic recording, nested connection creation, and the map are still open.

Study Trail’s Connect menu can now continue from an existing connection as well as start at a stop. It writes the original parent ID and chain depth, rejects parents from another stop or trail, and scopes duplicate detection to the immediate parent. Rows display the preceding destination. All 136 tests and both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA connected Genesis 2:1 via H1286 and verified the displayed Via H1286 label. Live iPhone, deeper UI chains and branch-map visualization remain open.

Study Trail adds a List/Map switch. The native map places connections beneath each stop in branch order, indents nested destinations, and opens passages, Lexicon entries, or notes directly. Missing legacy parents fall back to the stop level; connections whose stop no longer exists remain visible. Traversal guards against cycles. Both Dev builds and all 136 tests passed. Live Mac scratch QA saw Genesis 2 → H1286 → Genesis 2:1, confirmed the nested row, and opened H1286 from Map. Live iPhone, large trails, and the original graph’s full layout and interaction controls remain open.

Study Trail now offers Record Chapters Automatically on Mac and iPhone for one selected trail during the current app session. It records foreground Scripture chapters after the original 1.2-second dwell, ignores Compare and verse-only changes, and honors Pause, Resume, and Finish. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA enabled the switch, opened Jude through Command-K, and found it recorded. Opened Obadiah while paused and found it absent, then resumed and verified Obadiah appeared. Turned the switch off after testing. Live iPhone, rapid navigation cancellation, relaunch behavior, and automatic reasons/branch classification remain open.

While automatic recording is enabled, opening a Scripture result in Cross References records the jump, including same-chapter jumps and Open in New Tab. Capture requires an already-recorded source stop in the same edition and a live trail; the saved connection includes the source verse pin and destination range. Repeating the same link from the same verse does not duplicate it. Both Dev builds and all 137 tests passed, including edition, pause, same-chapter and distinct origin-verse coverage. Live Mac scratch QA opened TSKe Psalms 137:7 from Obadiah 1:1 and saw the Cross reference connection from Obadiah in the trail. Live iPhone, first-jump capture before the source dwell and automatic Lexicon/note connection capture remain open.

With automatic recording enabled, inline Strong’s Open and Open in New Tab now capture a Lexicon connection from the matching recorded Scripture stop. Both TextKit readers supply the originating verse, and the service writes the legacy click depth and reason while deduplicating repeated clicks from the same verse. Both Dev builds and all 138 tests passed. Live Mac scratch QA opened the G2316 link in Genesis 2 LXX, confirmed the definition appeared, and saw the new connection from Genesis 2. Live iPhone, context-menu routing and chained follow-up Lexicon navigation remain open.

Study Trail now has Find in This Trail for List and Map. It matches reference labels, the source verse, stop notes, connection notes, and reasons. Map filtering keeps matching branches with their parent context. Both Dev builds passed. Live Mac scratch QA searched for Psalms 137:7 and saw its source Obadiah 1:1, filtered Map to Genesis 2:1 and saw H1286 retained, and verified the no-results state. Live iPhone search and cross-trail search remain open.

Trail Actions now offers Rename Trail and Add/Edit Recap from List or Map. Rename updates the legacy title, and recap edits use the existing recap field and user-edited marker. The list renders the recap as Markdown and routes Berean links internally. All 138 tests and both final Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA saved a Markdown recap, verified the heading and body in the trail, and renamed the same trail without losing the recap or stops. The fixture is now named “Study Trail note QA — Recap”; future UI checks can find it by the same prefix. Live iPhone actions and recap links remain to check.

The full Lexicon tab now uses a circled Info symbol with native toolbar sizing on Mac and iPhone. On the Mac Dev build, clicked Info beside the Find, Search, and Copy controls and verified that the entry guide opened and dismissed. Live iPhone appearance remains to check.

The Study Trail library now searches saved names, recaps, Scripture references, Strong’s numbers, linked note titles, and stop and connection annotations. A result opens the matching trail with its contents filtered, and keeps a separate Find in This Trail field. No schema change. Both final Dev builds passed, all 138 Swift tests passed, and the focused migrated SQLite fixture with the new search assertions passed. On Mac Dev with the isolated QA library, searched for Psalms 137:7, opened the result, and verified the filtered connection from Obadiah 1:1 under Find in This Trail. Next check the iPhone layout and return to the library during an active search.

The Study Trail library supports Move Up and Move Down from a saved trail’s context menu. The actions are available in the full library, where neighboring rows are visible. They save dense positions to the legacy sort_order field in one transaction and exclude the loose-stop bucket. Both Dev builds and all 138 Swift tests passed. Mac scratch QA created “Ordering QA,” moved it below the existing recap trail, closed Study Trail, reopened it, and confirmed the order persisted. Long-press interaction on iPhone remains to check. Next implementation slice is Study Trail tags and separate sticky notes in the legacy tables.

Trail Actions → Tags now creates and assigns a tag, toggles any existing tag for the current trail, and includes assigned tag names in library search. Tag-only results open the whole trail. The code uses legacy trail_tags and trail_tag_members rows and reuses names case-insensitively. Both Dev builds and all 139 Swift tests passed. Mac scratch QA created “Trail Wisdom QA,” saw it assigned, searched for the tag in the library, and opened the full recap trail. The fixture verified removal and isolation across trails. Live iPhone interaction, tag colors, rename/delete, and tag-based grouping remain open. For the next sticky-note slice, use trail_notes in recorded migration 39 and compare src/mobile/trail/TrailNotesSheet.tsx for section and annotation editing, including anchoring to the latest stop.

Trail Actions → Sticky Notes now lists annotations and section headers and supports adding, editing, and deleting them. New entries attach to the latest stop or stay unanchored in an empty trail. Edits preserve legacy placement and other metadata. Deletion is scoped to the trail and clears its section collapse row in the same transaction. All 140 Swift tests and both final Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA found that primary toolbar actions were hidden in this nested sheet, so Add moved to the visible footer. On the rebuilt Mac app, created a section, edited its title, and verified that its body and Psalm 137 anchor stayed intact. Live iPhone interaction and inline map placement are still open.

Study Trail Map places anchored section headers immediately above the stop and annotations below it. Notes with no matching stop stay visible in Unanchored Sticky Notes. Sticky titles and bodies are searchable in the library and within List and Map. A note match retains its stop in Map. Both Dev builds and all 140 Swift tests passed. Mac scratch QA confirmed a saved section appeared above Psalm 137, that searching its body kept the anchor visible, and that library search opened the filtered note. The iPhone 18 Pro Simulator installed and launched the Dev build. The simulator window was unavailable to UI automation, so touch interaction remains unverified. Reanchoring, dragging and the legacy graph layout remain open.

Saved sticky notes and section headers can move between recorded passages or become unanchored. Map and List show an ellipsis button beside each note. The Sticky Notes manager offers the same commands in its row context menu. The destination includes the text edition and disambiguates repeated stops. The service updates the original anchor and order fields, refuses cross-trail moves, and preserves note content. All 140 Swift tests and both final Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA confirmed the visible map control and accessibility label. The automation did not activate the row menu, so live move and detach still need a check on Mac and iPhone. Drag placement remains open.

The iPhone Scripture dock now floats over a full-height reader, and the TextKit scroll inset reserves its height only while the dock is visible. The verse action capsule uses the same clearance and settles into the freed space when the dock hides. Normal non-reader screens and Compare still use the root safe-area dock. Scroll-driven chrome transitions respect Reduce Motion. Both Dev builds passed and the iPhone 18 Pro Simulator launched the new build. A Dev deep link to Romans 8:37–39 visually confirmed one rounded dashed group, a faint fill, and the complete last verse above both floating capsules. The scroll-driven hide/show, interactive selection, keyboard and physical-device red-letter glass checks remain open.

The next-chapter pull cue now uses the same bottom clearance as the floating reader controls, placing it above the iPhone dock. The iPhone TextKit coordinator resets its accumulated scroll direction and chrome state when a passage changes and calculates its end threshold in scroll-view coordinates. Chapter-change chrome restoration also respects Reduce Motion. Both Dev builds passed. On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, Dev links opened Romans 8:37–39 and then Romans 9:1–3. The latter displayed the new chapter’s three-verse outline with both floating controls visible. The link check did not exercise a drag or hidden-dock transition. Verify a bottom-edge pull after scrolling the dock away, a canceled pull, and the next chapter’s first scroll.

When a verse is added to the selection in place, the Mac and iPhone Scripture readers now try to keep its entire contiguous selected group between the top chrome and the floating controls. If the group is taller than that space, the reader tries to reveal the verse the user selected. Disjoint groups stay separate, and clearing a selection does not trigger a reveal. Both final Dev builds and nine focused Swift assertions passed. The assertions covered selection extension in both directions, disjoint ranges, tapped-endpoint priority, and visibility calculations. Live click, shift-click and touch verification of the reveal path remains open.

The iPhone Scripture reader now classifies taps before claiming them. UIKit receives linked glyphs and active text selections, including verse marker, Strong’s, note and tag links. Body and unlinked gutter taps still toggle verses. Chapter-turn taps are limited to the empty outer margin, which shrinks in wider reading layouts. The iPhone Dev build passed and the iPhone 18 Pro Simulator displayed the selected range John 3:16–18 with one dashed outline and floating actions. The range was opened through a Dev link. Live tap routing, link activation and selection handles remain to verify.

The iPhone Scripture action bar and dock now use a stronger adaptive backing behind their Liquid Glass. The original planned checks reproduced readable red glyphs through the dock. Before-and-after iPhone 18 Pro Simulator captures of John 3:1–2 showed that the backing suppresses those glyphs in Light Mode, and a Dark Mode capture showed clear icons over the same passage. Restored Light Mode after capture. Both Dev builds passed after correcting the backing modifier’s generic SwiftUI view type. Physical iPhone, landscape, and Reduce Transparency remain open in IOS-UI-030.

The Mac TextKit Scripture reader now uses one cursor decision for tracking-area updates and mouse movement, based on the same glyph and verse-gutter hit tests as clicking. Links use the pointing hand and ordinary text uses the I-beam. The hover fill stays on visible link characters, including when a verse number has an enlarged gutter target. The Mac Dev build passed and the isolated Mac app opened Romans 8:37–39 with the grouped outline, inline Strong’s and floating actions visible. The test did not measure cursor transitions. Verify slow movement across the verse gutter, link glyphs, whitespace and normal text, and confirm the cursor exits cleanly into the toolbar or study pane.

The Mac floating verse action bar now retains Copy, Highlight, Tag, and Clear when the full row cannot fit. Less frequent actions move behind More Verse Actions. Both normal Dev builds passed. In a temporary Mac scratch build with a 320-point action bar proposal, opened Romans 8:37–39, confirmed direct Tag, and opened More to verify Copy Reference, Add Note, Cross References, Save, and Share. Escape dismissed the menu and kept the range selected. The 320-point constraint was test-only. A live split-pane resize across the breakpoint remains unverified.

The iPhone Scripture dock and verse actions now share one row in landscape when at least 580 points of reader width are available. The reader reserves one row, then returns to stacked controls in narrower layouts. Both normal Dev builds passed. The iPhone 18 Pro Simulator used a scratch build with a launch-only rotation request. In landscape, a fresh Jude 1:22–25 link showed the full selected range above the two readable capsules. A previously saved Romans 8 selection reopened during rotation with verse 39 partly clipped, and John 3:34–36 reused an earlier viewport. Audit restored reading positions versus explicit verse requests separately. Rotation requests were excluded from the committed build. Direct touch actions and physical-device landscape remain to check.

Explicit verse navigation now carries its pending request through reader mounting and chapter loading, scoped to the destination tab and chapter. A pending request takes precedence over a saved viewport. The iPhone reader completes TextKit chapter layout before clamping an explicit jump to the scrollable end. The initial iPhone build still left John 3:34–36 offscreen after an earlier John 3 visit. With the TextKit fix, iPhone 18 Pro Simulator opened John 3:1–2 and then John 3:34–36, showing the complete final three verses grouped above both capsules. Both relevant Dev builds passed. Reopening the same saved range during landscape rotation and ordinary tab-return scroll restoration remain to verify.

iPhone Scripture now restores saved positions after TextKit has a nonzero viewport. Same-width restoration uses the saved offset. When width changes it uses the saved verse and its position relative to the top, and chapter-end stops remain at the end. The first simulator rotation run reproduced the clipped range. A temporary diagnostic run recorded a zero-width viewport during restoration, before SwiftUI laid out the returning tab. After the fix, a temporary launch script opened Romans 8:37–39 in portrait, switched to a different tab, rotated to landscape, and returned to the original tab. All three verses and the complete final line were visible above the controls. Both Dev builds passed. Same-width touch navigation and live rotation while staying in a reader remain to check. Temporary rotation and logging code was excluded from the committed source.

The iPhone reader now remembers the last reading anchor at the old width during an in-place rotation and reapplies it after the viewport settles. If the user starts scrolling or opens a different target, the late restore yields to that interaction. Baseline iPhone 18 Pro Simulator QA reproduced Romans 8:39 clipping below the floating controls when rotating while Romans 8:37–39 remained selected. Restoring at the first new width still clipped a line. Waiting for the height and safe-area changes restored the full range above both capsules. The normal iPhone Dev build passed and was reinstalled after returning the simulator to portrait. Rotation was triggered by a temporary launch script, excluded from the branch. Ordinary mid-chapter and reverse-direction rotation still need checks.

The follow-up in-place rotation check found that portrait to landscape could clip the first line of a previously visible mid-chapter selection. The reader now records whether the contiguous group was fully visible before rotation and keeps that same group clear of the bars when it fits after reflow. The adjustment preserves the regular reading anchor if the group was offscreen. A temporary iPhone 18 Pro Simulator rotation script verified Romans 8:17–19 with every line visible in landscape after the fix, and again when rotating back to portrait. The normal Dev build passed and was reinstalled. Touch-driven and physical-device rotation remain unverified.

The verse action overflow on iPhone and compact Mac now offers Compare Selected Verses, or Compare This Verse for a single selection. It opens Compare in the same tab with the existing range or disjoint verse state. Verse-specific context actions on both platforms now write an explicit single-verse passage so a previously saved disjoint selection cannot leak into Compare. Both normal Dev builds passed. In a temporary 320-point Mac QA build, selected Genesis 1:1–3, opened the overflow, chose Compare Selected Verses, and verified KJVA and LXX columns with all three verse rows selected. Direct iPhone activation, disjoint selection and the single-verse stale-state fix still need live checks.

Aligned Compare now toggles individual verse rows as a multi-selection. It writes contiguous ranges and disjoint groups to the same tab state as the Scripture reader. Mac Shift-click extends from the local anchor. Both Dev builds passed. In the Mac scratch app, opened Genesis 1:1–3 in Compare, deselected verse 2, verified verses 1 and 3 stayed selected, then switched Compare off and confirmed the reader showed Genesis 1:1, 3. The automation’s modifier click behaved like an ordinary click, so physical Shift-click and iPhone tap interaction remain to verify. Independent comparison columns were outside this change.

Independently scrolling Compare columns now show the reader’s selected verses in each following edition, including a faint row fill and the accessibility selected trait. Selection maps through the existing chapter/verse counterpart rules, and missing verses or independently pinned passages do not get a substitute selection. A column without a saved scroll position starts at its first mapped selected verse. Collapsing a mixed Compare to a following column can recover the selection from that column’s standalone rows. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA disabled Sync Scrolling with Genesis 1:1, 3 selected and verified both columns marked exactly 1 and 3. A subsequent Genesis 2:20–22 check marked all three rows in both columns. Exact initial visual placement, pinned and remapped passages, one-column handoff, and iPhone interaction remain to check.

Aligned Compare now loads equivalent chapters and aligns known KJV–Brenton verse counterparts for more than Jonah. A verified counterpart takes precedence over an equal verse number. Brenton Joel 3 uses only the verified Joel 2 excerpt, and Psalm and Malachi chapter joins can load the additional chapter. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA verified that the selected KJVA Psalm 10:1 row contains Brenton Psalm 9:22. The reverse row was outside the portion exposed by Mac UI automation, so reverse alignment, Joel, Malachi, Jonah regression, and live iPhone remain to check.

Aligned Compare now identifies both contributing chapters below an edition heading when its rows cross a chapter boundary. Pinning a column is respected even if its book and chapter still match the main passage. Changes in pin state invalidate the aligned layout while the existing scroll-position transfer carries unchanged destinations forward. Both Dev builds passed. In Mac scratch QA, Brenton Psalm 113 showed the KJVA heading “Psalm 114 · Psalm 115” and aligned Brenton 113:9 with KJVA 115:1. Pinning the KJVA column at Psalm 114 immediately opened independent panes. Follow Main Passage restored the aligned heading. Live iPhone, Joel and Malachi remain open.

Opening a selected verse or mapped range from Compare now queues an explicit Scripture jump, so an old standalone reader offset cannot win over the chosen destination. When only standalone panel rows are available, the handoff also prefers the mapped active verse over the first selected row. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA opened Brenton Psalm 113:9–10 in aligned Compare, chose Open in Reader for the KJVA column, and verified Scripture opened Psalm 115:1–2 with both verses selected. The single-chapter reader retains the part of a mapping in the destination anchor chapter. Background tab return, disjoint independently scrolled handoffs, and direct iPhone activation remain to check.

Open in Reader now carries the visible column position when no verse is selected. For aligned chapter joins, it uses the chapter of the displayed row; independently scrolling columns use their own saved top verse. It seeds the reader viewport without selecting a verse, and an explicit selection continues to take precedence. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA scrolled unselected Brenton Psalm 113 Compare through the KJVA Psalm 115 rows, chose the KJVA column, and verified Scripture opened Psalm 115 with no verse selection. The exact first visible line, independent and stacked-column behavior, and iPhone still need live verification.

Stacked Compare now tracks the reading verse per column in the shared scroll view. Opening a stacked column without a verse selection uses that column’s tracked verse, with its first verse as the fallback before its section is reached. Both Dev builds passed. An iPhone 18 Pro Simulator fixture opened three columns with one pinned to Genesis 2, scrolled the first Genesis 1 column, and invoked the same Open in Reader handler used by the menu. The fixture logged Genesis 1:9 as the anchor. The Scripture screen opened with verse 9 first and no selection. The fixture was removed from the scratch build. Direct touch-menu activation and jumping back up between stacked sections remain to check.

Stacked Compare anchors now come from rows still visible in the chosen column. Lazy rows unregister on disappearance, so jumping back up cannot reuse a later row that left the viewport. Both Dev builds passed. A temporary iPhone 18 Pro simulator fixture scrolled the stacked Genesis 1 column down to verse 18, back up to verse 7, then invoked Open in Reader. The reader showed verse 7 first with no selection. Direct touch-menu activation remains to check. The launch fixture was excluded from the branch.

Aligned Compare now uses currently visible rows for its reading anchor in both grid and stacked layouts. Lazy rows unregister when they disappear. Turning Sync Scrolling off maps that anchor into each edition, and turning it back on resolves the independent column’s local verse to its aligned row when available. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA scrolled the aligned Brenton Psalm 113 comparison and verified Open in Reader still opened its visible KJVA Psalm 115 portion without a selection. Also confirmed that disabling Sync produces independent panes for KJVA Psalm 10 and Brenton Psalm 9. Exact scroll alignment through both sync toggles and iPhone interaction remain to check.

Live independent Compare sync now resolves verified verse counterparts and otherwise synchronizes equal canonical chapters of the same book. Columns covering unrelated passages stay put. This follows the original app's passage grouping and improves on its raw verse matching at known chapter joins. Five focused mapping assertions and the full 141-test Swift run passed, followed by both Dev builds. The Mac scratch app initially moved KJVA only on wheel input. A Mac wheel driver fallback made a downward wheel scroll move Brenton Psalm 9 from its opening verses to the corresponding portion alongside KJVA Psalm 10. The upward follow-up still did not move the Brenton pane. Bidirectional wheel propagation, touch input, and a pinned unrelated-pane UI check remain open.

Mac independent Compare now identifies the pane under wheel input before SwiftUI publishes position changes, then maps the first actually visible source row. The follower’s position updates cannot claim wheel ownership. Mac scratch QA scrolled KJVA Psalm 10 down and back up with Sync Scrolling enabled. The Brenton pane advanced and returned to Psalm 9:22 alongside KJVA Psalm 10:1, confirmed visually because the accessibility tree also includes cached offscreen rows. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone touch and unrelated pinned-pane UI checks remain open.

Mac floating search now resolves Return and Shift-Return against the field's current query, even when the results view still reflects the previous input. Selection index and Notes hits are scoped to the query. A fast submission resolves exact note titles even if the Notes result task has not run yet. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA pasted Genesis 4:1–3 and pressed Return immediately, then repeated the sequence with the synthetic note “Highlight palette scratch.” Both opened the intended target. Shift-Return and rapid Arrow-key navigation still need live checks.

The iPhone Go field now takes focus after presentation. Keyboard Go resolves the current query immediately, including an exact title among its Notes matches, with the same tab-reuse choice as the displayed result rows. The iPhone 18 Pro simulator showed the insertion cursor and opened a synthetic note from Scripture when the fixture submitted its title before the results task ran. The simulator used its hardware-keyboard configuration, so the on-screen keyboard was not verified.

The full Lexicon tab's Entry Guide uses a body-size icon and a standard 32-point Mac or 44-point iPhone control area within its existing toolbar group. The compact inspector uses the same label and keeps its inline button style. The iPhone 18 Pro simulator showed the control alongside the other entry actions. Mac Dev scratch QA clicked the info button and dismissed the guide with Done. Verify guide presentation and dismissal with iPhone touch.

Cross-reference previews on both platforms show an inline Show More control when the text actually exceeds three lines at the current panel width. Show Less restores the compact row. The reference header opens the passage, and the existing ellipsis and context menus also expose Open. In a Mac Dev scratch panel at 310 points wide, Genesis 1:1 → TSKe showed the control for Mark 13:19 but not for the shorter adjacent preview. Clicking it exposed the final line, and Show Less collapsed it. The fixture was excluded from the branch. Next verify iPhone touch expansion and long-press, and review card layout and VoiceOver navigation.

Cross-reference rows now use a lazy scroll stack so macOS exposes the passage button, preview text, Show More or Show Less, and the action menu as separate accessibility elements. Section headings are exposed as headings, and preview expansion respects Reduce Motion. Both Dev targets built in the scratch checkout. Mac accessibility automation activated Mark 13:19's Show More and Show Less, observed the state change on the button, and opened its menu to verify Open, Copy, and Open in New Tab were exposed. VoiceOver speech, keyboard traversal, and iPhone touch still need direct checks. TSKe range previews still show only the first verse from the corpus, so full range previews are a separate parity gap.

TSKe and Classic range rows now offer Show More even when the opening verse fits. Expanding a row loads its destination chapter on demand, keeps only the referenced verse range, and restores the original first-verse preview on collapse. My Notes continues to expand the note excerpt. Mac Dev scratch QA expanded Proverbs 8:22–24 from Genesis 1:1 → TSKe and observed all three verses in the inline preview. Next check touch expansion in the iPhone sheet and sweep the other Cross Refs sources.

iPhone 18 Pro Simulator interaction is available through Xcode's Device Hub (open `/Applications/Xcode.app/Contents/Applications/DeviceHub.app`; the former Simulator.app path is absent on this host). With the iOS 27 Dev build, tapped Lexicon Info and Done, opened Scripture through Go's recent passages, opened Cross Refs for John 3:34–36, and expanded Verse 34 by tapping its row away from the caret. Show More displayed the full John 8:26–28 range. A long press opened the Open, Copy and Open in New Tab menu. Open dismissed the sheet, scrolled the reader to John 8:26, and selected verses 26–28 within one dashed outline. Physical device, VoiceOver, clipboard and the Classic/My Notes touch paths remain open. In this run, the reader action capsule still visually overlapped the middle of verse 33; follow up on reading clearance while the action bar and dock are both visible.

iPhone Scripture now shortens the scroll viewport while verse actions are visible. The glass action bar and dock stay floating below the readable area. With selection cleared, the viewport returns to the dock-only layout. The iOS Dev build passed. On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, reopened John 8:26–28 and confirmed the former overlap at verse 33 was gone. Tapping verse 33 at the lower edge scrolled all its lines above the action bar, retained the separate 26–28 outline, and clearing selection brought more text into view. Landscape, chapter-end clearance and the corresponding Mac action-bar layout still need direct verification.

Mac Scripture now reserves real reading space below the scroll viewport while the verse action bar is visible. Selection reveal and reading-position capture use the shortened AppKit clip bounds directly. Mac Dev built successfully. In the scratch reader, ⌘K → John 8:26–28 showed the grouped outline and a clear bottom action bar, with verse text ending above it. John 8:59 fit fully above the bar at the end of the chapter, and Escape removed the bar. The iPhone portrait check is recorded above. iPhone landscape and chapter-end behavior, and short Mac window sizing, remain open.

The owner clarified that the Scripture action bar should overlay scrolling text. Restored the floating overlay and full-height viewport on both platforms. The scroll inset and selection reveal use the bar's height so the last lines can scroll clear. This supersedes the preceding viewport-shortening changes. During the superseded layout's QA, iPhone 18 Pro showed John 8:58–59 fully visible in portrait and after rotating to landscape, with the bar and dock side-by-side. Mac's Top tile exposed a separate resize issue: an already-visible selection could leave the viewport when tiled. Reproduce that on the restored overlay before bringing back any resize-specific change.

With floating overlays restored, macOS Fill → Top tile still moved the selected John 8:58–59 below the readable viewport. The Mac reader now rechecks a previously visible selected run after resizing and scrolls it clear of the action bar. It considers the focused verse if the full run cannot fit. Mac Dev built successfully and the same tiling sequence kept both verses and their shared outline visible. Very large selections and horizontal resizing still need direct checks.

Mac Scripture cursor tracking now checks the window's frontmost hit view before setting a cursor or drawing link hover feedback. This lets the floating SwiftUI controls own the pointer when they overlap the AppKit reader. Mac Dev built successfully. A direct moving-pointer check across the capsule, verse-number gutter, inline Strong’s links, and ordinary text is pending.

Mac Compare's Manage Translations and the full Lexicon toolbar now explicitly use the shared clickable pointer, with adjacent-entry buttons using the arrow when disabled. The Compare column, occurrence filter, and Cross Refs source menus use a single pointer modifier. Both Dev targets built. Mac scratch QA opened the Lexicon guide and Compare's Manage Translations menu after these changes. Cursor glyph motion remains to verify directly.

iPhone Go now shows a plain-text excerpt under each matching note title, using the same match-centered excerpt as Notes. On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, entering `synthetic` displayed two test notes whose titles omit the term and showed their matching body text. Tapping the first result dismissed Go and opened that note without increasing the workspace's 16-tab count. Passage result previews and physical-device checks remain open.

iPhone Go passage results now show a two-line excerpt sourced from the resolved edition. A verse or range previews its first requested verse, and a chapter previews its first numbered verse. Results only render text for the currently resolved passage. In the iPhone 18 Pro simulator, entered Jude 5, replaced it with Isaiah 53 LXX, verified both excerpts and the Septuagint edition label, then tapped the second result. Go dismissed and opened Isaiah 53 LXX while the workspace stayed at 16 tabs. Mac floating search still has its separate presentation.

Mac floating search now shows a single-line Scripture excerpt below the direct passage result. It uses the same edition-aware loader as iPhone Go, and only displays the excerpt when its resolved passage matches the result. The field and results viewport retain fixed heights. Mac scratch QA replaced Jude 5 with Isaiah 53 LXX, verified the excerpt and edition changed while the field held focus, then pressed Return to open Isaiah 53 in Brenton. Both Dev targets built. Hover and Shift-Return with the taller result row remain to check.

Mac floating search now shows a one-line body excerpt for both exact-title and other Notes hits. Excerpts are generated once per query, using the Notes and iPhone Go formatter. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA searched for the body-only phrase `color test`, observed the excerpt, moved down to the result, and pressed Shift-Return. The note opened in a new tab, taking the scratch workspace from 12 to 13 tabs. Pointer hover remains to verify.

Floating search now keeps an explicit keyboard or hover selection by result ID and query. Result insertion can move the row without changing which action Return invokes. A new query defaults to its first row, and arrow events from a stale rendered query cannot select a row in the new one. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA selected Search Notes with the arrow keys and verified that Return opened Notes. The delayed-result fixture did not capture the list before reordering, so an observed insertion while a row is selected still needs a direct check.

iPhone Go and Mac floating search now preview a recognized Strong's entry with its headword, transliteration and gloss (definition as fallback). The lookup stays available when the corpus has no preview. Preview loading is scoped to the normalized Strong's ID, and neither surface delays opening an entry for the excerpt. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA replaced G3056 with H3068, verified the Greek and Hebrew previews updated, and pressed Return to open H3068. iPhone interaction and missing-entry behavior remain to check.

On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, Go showed G3056's Greek preview, cleared it when replaced by H9999, and opened the missing-entry state without adding a tab. Searching H3068 then showed its Hebrew preview and opened the valid entry in the same tab. The initial six-control toolbar truncated the identifier at portrait width. The iPhone full Lexicon now groups Find in Entry, Search Lexicon and Copy Definition under Entry Tools, with entry stepping and Info directly available.

The updated portrait Lexicon showed H3068 in the title and a four-control toolbar. Tapping Entry Tools displayed all three commands, and Find in Entry opened its field. Smaller iPhone widths, the compact title after scrolling, hardware shortcuts and clipboard output still need checks.

The iPhone full Lexicon now uses an inline navigation title, keeping the Strong’s identifier in the toolbar and starting the entry body with the headword. iOS Dev built successfully. The iOS 27 iPhone 17e simulator opened H3068 using the Dev deep link, showed its full number beside the four toolbar controls, and opened Info. Device Hub scroll input did not move the simulated entry in this check. Larger Dynamic Type, scrolling, and physical-device checks remain open.

Lexicon occurrences now show individually removable edition, book, translated-word, and sort chips. Clearing a chip preserves the other settings and resets pagination. Zero filtered matches have an explicit empty state. The regular build stalled reading its cache, so both Dev targets were verified in the independent scratch checkout with the updated StudyViews source. Mac scratch QA on G3056 applied LXX and Genesis, cleared LXX while Genesis stayed active, then cleared Genesis and saw All shown. iPhone chip interactions and the filtered-empty state remain to test directly.

Lexicon occurrence searches now detect truncation per edition by probing one extra row, while keeping the existing result limits and ordering. The UI marks a capped total with a plus, distinguishes loaded results in filter summaries, and says Show All Loaded where appropriate. Mac scratch QA verified G3056 at 811+ (one edition is capped) and the exact count of 1 for H1286. Both scratch Dev builds and all seven focused Lexicon tests passed, including the exact-limit test and occurrence conformance. The regular SwiftPM cache could not load its build manifest, so it did not provide current-source verification. The next parity gap is filtering common entries across occurrences beyond the current sample.

Full Lexicon entries now load every matching verse in the supported tagged editions for book, edition, and rendering filters. The compact inspector still loads its capped sample. The full query retrieves verse text in the same SELECT and deduplicates repeated LXX destinations, keeping the first matching row. Existing capped service output still passes the legacy goldens. Both scratch Dev builds and eight focused Lexicon tests passed. Mac scratch QA found 5,517 occurrences for H3068 and filtered to 141 in Genesis, matching an independent corpus query. iPhone interaction and common-entry performance still need direct checks.

Lexicon now prepares occurrence snippets and translated-word keys off the main actor. Initial loading publishes the rows with their prepared previews. Preference changes reformat the current entry with cancellation and stale-entry checks. Both scratch builds passed with the updated StudyViews and LexiconService files. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator opened H3068 and stepped to H3069, which showed its own 291 occurrences. These were slice builds in the older scratch checkout. The whole current-branch integration build, timing measurements, rapid stepping during a pending format pass, and a live word-replacement toggle remain open.

Full current-source verification for `b1ce11d0` passed from the repository checkout using fresh /tmp caches: Mac and generic iOS Simulator Debug builds, both Dev bundle identifiers, 139 Swift Testing cases and four XCTest cases. The iPhone 18 Pro Simulator ran that integrated build, opened H3068 with the inline title, stepped to H3069 (291 occurrences), chose Genesis (2 results), and cleared the visible chip to restore all results. Physical-device timing and the word-replacement/race checks remain open. GitHub read-only review at this checkpoint showed #22 open against main and #23 open against native/m7-export, both green. Draft #24 targeted native/m6-navigation with native CI queued at the inspected head. Preserve the review and retarget order, and recheck CI after a push.

The Mac and iPhone readers preserve their current scroll offset when floating verse controls add bottom clearance. The selection reveal can still move an overlapping selection above the capsule, and the last verse can scroll clear of the controls. iPhone reduces the extra chapter-end text padding from 24 to 10 points. Both current-source Dev builds passed. An AppKit probe confirmed that the scroll viewport stays full-height, adding a 46-point inset preserves the old offset and adds 46 points of scroll travel, and removing the inset clamps back to the original end. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator showed Genesis 1:25–26 selected with verses 29–30 continuing beneath both capsules. Next live QA: select and clear a wrapped final verse on Mac and iPhone, and verify hand scrolling under the capsule with large text and in landscape.

Full Lexicon occurrence lists now follow the shared Scripture book order within each edition. Chapter and verse order and deterministic duplicate handling are preserved. The compact inspector keeps the legacy capped results. Eight focused Lexicon tests passed, including both editions of G3056, H3068 starting at Genesis 2:4, and the legacy occurrence goldens. Runtime follow-up: on Mac and iPhone open H3068 with default order, then filter to a late book and clear the filter to check the return to Genesis.

The Scripture-order Lexicon change passed both current-source Dev builds and the full BereanKit suite (139 conformance cases plus four workspace tests). The full debug run took about two minutes.

Lexicon edition, book, and translated-word filters now combine on both platforms. An active choice remains visible and removable at zero matches, with its zero count available in the picker. The full Lexicon names its default sort Scripture Order. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator verified G3056 → 1 Chronicles (18 LXX rows) → KJVA (zero, with both chips visible) → clear KJVA (18 rows again, with the book chip retained). Live Mac and translated-word intersection checks remain open.

Inline Strong’s lookups now pass the clicked verse into the existing device-local History entry on Mac and iPhone. History displays and filters Strong’s lookup rows by their source passage, including the verse when present. Related Lexicon entries opened via Open in New Tab also register the original tab for Back and dismiss the compact iPhone inspector.
