Both current-source Dev builds and the two focused history store tests passed. An existing process held the Mac QA library, so live checks are pending. Next: in a free isolated Dev library, open an inline Strong’s entry from a known verse, search History → Lexicon for that reference, then open a related entry in a new tab and use Back.

New Strong’s History rows capture the source edition alongside the book, chapter, and verse, for both single-edition Scripture and the clicked Compare column. The history row still opens the Lexicon entry. Its context menu adds Open Source Passage, which visits the recorded edition and registers History for Back. Legacy rows without a saved edition still display their known passage context and open the Lexicon entry. The existing device-local query field carries a stable, sorted-key payload and needs no schema migration. Both Dev build commands exited successfully. Live check pending: switch a Compare column to LXX, open a tagged word there, then use History → Open Source Passage and Back.

Strong’s History uses the same row-restoration path for Open Source Passage as for opening a History result. Its context menu explicitly distinguishes Open Lexicon Entry and Open Source Passage. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator verified Genesis 1:1 LXX → G2316 → History → Open Source Passage → Back, with the G2316 row visible again. A deeply scrolled row, the Compare-specific route and Mac interaction remain to check.

Lexicon Occurrences now groups a leading, labeled Filters button and Search Scripture action above a single results count in both the full entry and inspector. Filters open a Mac popover or compact iPhone sheet, with a searchable book list and searchable translated-word choices. Existing filter chips and filters that combine are preserved. The shared hover treatment covers Lexicon choices and rows, plus Advanced Search Options, Scope, and option controls. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator opened H3069, applied Amos in the new panel, and showed 20 of 291 results with the Amos chip. That check preceded the iPhone sheet top-alignment adjustment. Live Mac hover, narrow inspector layout, and translated-word search are still to verify.

Both current-source Mac and iPhone Simulator Dev builds passed after the hover and sheet-alignment refinements.

Advanced Scripture Search gives the query its own full-width row, with the current scope, labeled Options, and any active tag chip grouped immediately below in a wrapping layout. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator verified a zero-match search kept the field and both controls at the top, and tapping Options opened the sheet with Match, Testament, Books, Verse Tags, Results, and Context visible. Live Mac pointer and narrow-window wrap checks remain open.

Advanced Search now shows removable chips for nondefault match mode, testament, books, and verse tags below the query. Clearing one category preserves the others. Both Dev builds passed. On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, searching grace showed 300 results, choosing New Testament showed 122 and a removable chip, and clearing it restored 300 without reopening Options. Mac interaction and the multi-filter layout at large Dynamic Type remain open.

Advanced Search distinguishes loaded counts from exact totals when the text search reports an edition limit. Capped edition chips carry a plus, the limit notice follows the focused edition, and the empty state distinguishes loaded results from an exhaustive search. The scope is shown once in the header. Both Dev build commands exited successfully. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator searching God showed 5,616 loaded, KJVA 2,000+ and LXX 2,000+, with the limit notice. Focused-edition switching and a capped empty filter remain to check. Strong’s-number search still needs its own completeness audit.

Strong’s Advanced Search and the restored-name bridge now use the complete Lexicon query. This replaces the high-cap sample path and its extra verse lookup per result, and uses the matching tagged row at duplicate destinations. Repeated Strong’s tokens scan once and preserve the same results as a single token. Fourteen focused Search and Lexicon tests and both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator showed 5,517 results for H3068, starting at Genesis 2:4. Mac live interaction, interrupted common queries and a combined-number query in the UI remain to verify.

Advanced Search now applies Testament to each edition’s book list before the text query’s result limit, intersecting with any explicit book filter. A nonmatching edition or incompatible book selection is skipped, and the restored-name bridge obeys the same scope. Changing Testament starts a new request. The focused corpus regression verifies that a New Testament search for God returns the same results as explicitly searching all KJVA New Testament books, recovers results beyond the unrestricted sample, and returns zero for a conflicting Genesis filter. Seven focused tests passed. Live UI verification for a capped term remains open.

Both current-source Dev builds passed for the Testament search change.

Text search now probes one extra matching row at its existing limits. Advanced Search no longer infers truncation from the displayed count, so an exact boundary stays exact. Any Word carries forward per-term limits and the unscoped merged-list limit. The verse search API preserves the existing row limits and order. Eighteen focused tests passed, including the original Bible search goldens, an exact filtered boundary, and over-limit All Words and Any Word queries. UI retest for this increment remains open.

Both Mac and iPhone Simulator Dev builds passed for the exact-limit change.

Compare is now directly available in the full Mac verse action capsule. Previously only its compact overflow variants exposed the selection-specific Compare command. The wide Mac button and compact Mac/iPhone menu share the action and keep the same selected verse state when switching reader modes. Both Dev builds passed. Live check pending: open Compare from a disjoint selection via the wide Mac capsule, repeat from a narrow pane, and confirm selection and Back on both paths.

Explicit Compare commands from Scripture now discard the stale aligned and independent-column Compare scroll positions for the source edition and chapter. Compare can then use the existing selection as its initial anchor. Standard Compare opens, Back/Forward, other chapters, and the reader's own scroll bookmark keep their existing paths. The stacked layout for independently pinned columns is outside this change. Live QA pending: open Compare, scroll far away, leave, tap a new verse and use its Compare action, then check the initial viewport and Back.

Both native Dev builds passed for the Compare jump change.

Stacked Compare now has column-qualified verse scroll targets. With a verse selection and no saved stacked offset, it opens on the focused selected verse in a following column, preferring the source edition when available. The initial jump runs in the same task as normal bookmark restoration. An explicit Compare command clears the matching chapter's stacked offset alongside aligned and independent-column positions. Pinned columns keep their own passages. Live check pending: pin a different passage in one column, force stacked layout, open Compare from a late verse, then check the anchored row and Back.

Both native Dev builds passed for stacked Compare navigation.

Compare History now records disjoint verse groups in its existing optional passage payload, using the same selection validation as Scripture. Reopening restores those groups and the active verse; a different group selection also distinguishes otherwise identical open Compare tabs. Older entries still decode. Live check pending: select two nonadjacent verses, open Compare, reopen its History entry, then confirm both verses are highlighted and that Back returns to History.

Both native Dev builds passed for Compare History verse groups.

New Compare History snapshots preserve independent scrolling alongside the existing columns, pinned passages, and verse groups. Reopening only reuses a matching Compare tab when its effective scrolling mode also matches. Older History payloads retain the synchronized default. Changing Sync Scrolling alone remains a presentation change and does not create a History row. Both native Dev builds passed. Live check pending: turn Sync Scrolling off, create a new Compare History entry by changing the passage, close the tab, and reopen the entry.

New Compare History entries capture tab-level Strong’s visibility and hidden text annotations. Reopening restores those options and distinguishes open Compare tabs that have different saved display options. Older entries decode with default display options. Global preferences such as verse-number visibility remain live preferences. Both Dev builds passed. Live combined verification: create a Compare History entry with Strong’s on, at least one text annotation hidden, and Sync Scrolling off; reopen the entry after closing its tab and verify all three options.

Native independent Compare panes now recognize a legacy column’s scrollPos.verseNum as a fallback when no native bookmark or selected verse supplies a target. They open at the saved verse or closest preceding available verse. Legacy fractional row offsets remain in the saved record for the original client. Live check pending: open a legacy workspace clone with a pinned column and a noninitial scrollPos, then verify the position in a native independent pane.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Stacked Compare now carries the first visible surviving column's verse across a column reorder or removal. It maps the column to its new index and restores by verse, since a raw vertical offset can land in a different section after reordering. Aligned Compare retains offset restoration. Both Dev build commands exited successfully. Live check pending: stack a comparison with a pinned passage, scroll within a column, move it, and verify that the same verse remains visible.

iPhone Go groups its existing shortcuts in an adaptive grid, with Scripture, Notes, History, and Lexicon first. Accessibility Dynamic Type uses a wider minimum column. Search/result and tab-reuse behavior use their existing routes. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator verified all seven shortcuts fit on the first screen with the keyboard closed, then opened History directly and confirmed Go dismissed. Large text and software-keyboard coverage remain to check.

iPhone Go now records a return to the source tab when a destination opens another tab in the same workspace. Today’s Note honors explicit New Tab while opening the same daily note through the existing date lookup. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator verified Genesis 1 → Go → History → More → Back returned to Genesis. The Today’s Note reuse-versus-new-tab interaction remains unverified.

Go’s passage result now shows Open Tab when the exact reference and edition already exist in the current workspace. That indicator uses the same match predicate as tab reuse and is omitted in explicit New Tab mode. Repeated cross-tab navigation from the same source no longer appends consecutive duplicate Back stops. Live checks pending: find an already-open passage in Go, verify reuse and the indicator, then repeat from the same source after manually switching tabs.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Mac floating search labels an exact open Scripture destination Switch to Tab in result rows and the footer, including recent passages. The hint uses the existing reuse predicate and is omitted in explicit New Tab and Command-L location-editing modes. Both Dev builds passed. Live checks pending: regular Command-K on an open passage, Shift-Return to open a new tab, and Command-L to keep editing the current reader.

Mac floating search now records a Back path whenever a result or command opens a different tab within the same workspace. This includes Switch to Tab, explicit New Tab, and archived restores into the same workspace. Same-tab edits continue through existing location history. Today’s Note also honors explicit New Tab. Both native Dev builds passed. Live Mac checks pending for Command-K → Switch to Tab → Back, Shift-Return → Back, and a workspace switch.

Workspace search on Mac and iPhone now accepts terms spread across multiple tab titles in one workspace. It still prefers a single tab title that explains the whole match. Mac sidebar and floating search show the relevant previews, matching the iPhone workspace list. Both Dev builds and standalone assertions against the production WorkspaceItem implementation passed, covering generated workspace names, edition labels, split-title matches, and missing terms. Live verification with duplicate-numbered workspaces remains open.

Workspace search on Mac and iPhone now keeps the matching results open when Return would be ambiguous between identical workspace names or identical generated passage labels. It shows a prompt to choose a workspace. A unique exact name still opens directly, and exact matching is case-insensitive. Standalone checks against the workspace matcher covered duplicate Session 8 names, duplicate John 1 display labels, and a unique Romans 8 label. Live keyboard checks remain open.

Both native Dev builds passed for the workspace collision fix.

Recent Tabs uses the workspace picker’s passage-based label for automatically named workspaces. Mac’s Return to command uses the tab’s full navigation label (including a selected range) and names the destination workspace when it differs from the current one. Both Dev builds passed. Live check pending: switch between two unnamed workspaces and verify Return to and Recent Tabs identify the destination before opening it.

Move to Workspace now labels destinations with a second open tab when available, the stored numbered name when different, and the tab count. It applies to the Mac sidebar, iPhone individual and bulk moves, and Tab Cleanup. Both Dev builds passed. Standalone assertions verified distinct labels for two Session 8 workspaces that share John 1 but contain different second tabs. Runtime menu truncation and move interactions remain to check.

Moving tabs to another workspace now chooses the first surviving tab after a moved active tab in each affected Space, then falls back to the preceding survivor. This fixes the case where earlier tabs in the same move shifted the old index past the intended neighbor. The iPhone bulk-move receipt uses the readable workspace label. Live check pending with several moved tabs surrounding a remembered active tab in a background Space.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Back and Forward can follow an existing tab-history link after either tab moves to another live workspace. The lookup keeps local tabs first, skips closed or archived tabs, and changes workspaces only when following an existing link. Same-tab navigation still uses its existing location stack. Live check pending: create a Go or floating-search Back link, move its source tab, then use Back and Forward to traverse both workspaces.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Explicitly opening a tab in another live workspace now registers the source tab for Back. This covers workspace/tab search, exact passage matches in Mac floating search and iPhone Go, and cross-workspace Recent Tabs. It uses the existing workspace-aware Back/Forward traversal and verifies that the source still belongs to the workspace it was opened from. Live cross-workspace Back then Forward remains to check.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Duplicating a workspace now opens the copy in the source workspace’s active or remembered Space. Saved snapshots use their recorded preferred Space, with the existing fallback for older snapshots or empty Spaces. The duplicate still remaps tab IDs, active tabs, origins, and mixed display order. Live check pending: duplicate a saved workspace recorded on Notes while currently reading Scripture.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Native saved workspace snapshots now keep History and Calendar alongside Scripture and other tabs. The mixed display order, selected utility tab, and preferred Space survive restore even if a tab ID collides. Both Dev builds and five focused tests passed, including the TypeScript-generated legacy snapshot goldens. The legacy client’s own snapshot parser still omits these native utility types. Live Save Workspace → reopen remains to verify.

Opening a single archived tab in another workspace now uses the existing cross-workspace Back/Forward route. This covers Restore to Source and the case where the same tab is already open there after another device restored it. Group restore still keeps the reader in place. The Restore to Source command now uses the workspace’s readable label. Live check pending: restore one result to its source, verify the remaining archive entries, and use Back to return.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Restoring into a workspace with no active tab now reselects the original active tab when it was part of that archive and is being restored to the same workspace. Older archive metadata falls back to the first restored tab. Batch restore reconciles the destination order once using the existing missing-neighbor placement rule. Both native Dev builds and three archive ordering/partial-restore tests passed. A live Archive All → Restore All check remains open.

New archive placements remember each Space’s selected tab. When restoring to the original workspace, a Space that has no active tab selects its archived selection if that tab is in the restore batch. Spaces with live selections keep them. Old metadata decodes with the existing first-tab fallback. Both native Dev builds passed. Live check pending: select a nonfirst tab in two Spaces, archive all, restore the group, and check both Space selections.

Native tab rendering and persistence now share display-order reconciliation. It keeps the first occurrence of each live ID, drops stale and repeated IDs, and appends missing live tabs in Space order. Sixteen focused workspace and archive tests passed, including the legacy workspace/tab goldens and a malformed-order regression. Live verification with a repaired workspace remains open.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Native Save Workspace now writes a reconciled mixed tab order, dropping stale and repeated IDs before serializing. Older malformed orders are repaired when restored into a live workspace. The low-level parser retains the legacy format behavior, including duplicate entries in the original TypeScript golden fixture. Both Dev builds and six focused tests passed, including native save and malformed restore checks. Live save/reopen remains to verify.

The iPhone Archived Tabs list now exposes Restore and Open Here and, when available, Restore and Open in [source workspace] on each row’s long press. The existing swipe action still restores to the source in the background and now requires tapping its button, so a full swipe does not activate it. iPhone Simulator Dev built. Live long-press, swipe, and cross-workspace Back remain to verify.

Mac Browse Archived Tabs now distinguishes Restore and Open Here, Restore and Open in the source workspace, and restore to that workspace in the background. The open actions dismiss the browser and use the existing return path when switching workspaces. The background action keeps the archive browser open. Mac Dev built successfully. Live context-menu and focus checks remain open.

Exact Duplicates Only now checks native reading bookmarks before proposing Scripture and Compare copies. Tabs with identical persisted state but different current-chapter reading positions or Compare scroll bookmarks remain outside the duplicate suggestions, including at action time. The general inactive-tab list can still be used for intentional cleanup. Both targets are covered by the shared implementation. Live check pending: open the same chapter twice, scroll to different verses, and confirm Exact Duplicates Only leaves both alone.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Exact Duplicates Only now also checks device-local bookmarks and browsing options for Search, Lexicon, Notes, History, and Calendar. Identical persisted tabs that return to different results, filters, folders, or dates stay out of automatic duplicate suggestions. The comparison is intentionally conservative: missing local state differs from an existing bookmark. Live checks remain open for two copies of a search scrolled to different rows and for a Lexicon filter difference.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Tab Cleanup now explains that exact duplicates share tab settings and saved browsing position. A suggested duplicate's context menu can open the copy that will remain, for review before cleanup. The move receipt uses the readable destination label, and an ineligible archive attempt is visible even while an earlier archive is still undoable. Live context-menu and receipt checks remain open.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Tab Cleanup’s archive receipt now checks whether the original workspace is still live. If that destination is unavailable, it explains why Undo is unavailable and offers an explicit Restore Here action. If the user switched workspaces and the original still exists, Undo continues to restore there. Live check pending for a receipt whose workspace is no longer live.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Archived group menus on Mac and iPhone now offer Restore All to Workspace. The destination list uses the existing passage-based move labels and restores directly into a live workspace without opening it. Choosing the original workspace applies its saved placement; another destination appends the restored tabs in archive order. The same restore plan handles tab identity collisions. Live check pending: restore a group to a third workspace and verify source, destination, and archive state.

Both native Dev builds passed.

Restore All to Workspace now reports any archived tabs already open in a different workspace. Those tabs stay in place and the remaining archive members restore normally under the existing global ID rules. Live verification with a synthetic partial collision remains open.

Both native Dev builds passed.

## Lexicon and Advanced Search controls

The full Lexicon entry and compact study inspector share the same Occurrences heading, count, leading Filters and Search Scripture actions. The filter panel groups narrowing choices ahead of Order and opens one searchable Book or Translated Words list at a time. Advanced Search uses compact labeled menu pickers on Mac and clearer hover fills through the shared option style. The Scripture search action still passes the source tab to history and starts an all-texts search.

Both native Debug builds passed. Mac live QA reached H430 but the window still displayed the older Occurrence Options layout while two Dev instances shared the bundle identifier. Relaunch the current build with `--qa-library` after isolating its window, then check pointer movement across the filter rows, a book and translated-word selection, Reset Filters, Search Scripture and Back. On iPhone Simulator, check the filter sheet with the keyboard up at a large text size. The wider app control placement audit remains open.

Mac live QA follow-up on `0dfb38e4`: after closing two stale scratch-library processes, the current Dev window showed H430 with the Occurrences heading and 2,242 passages. The Filters popover opened and choosing 1 Kings narrowed the results to 90. Reset Filters restored the all-books count. Search Scripture opened Search: H430, whose Options popover exposed the new labeled Match, Order, Group by and Context pickers. Toolbar Back returned to H430. Pointer-only hover, typed filter queries, the compact inspector and iPhone large-text/keyboard layout still need separate checks.

## Empty workspace recovery

Mac and iPhone now offer Go and, when saved tabs are available, Browse Archived Tabs from the empty workspace. Mac presents the archive sheet at the window root, so the same archive browser is reachable from the sidebar and from an empty workspace while the sidebar is hidden. iPhone reuses its existing Archived Tabs page in a dismissible sheet. The empty state uses the existing restore operations.

Both Dev Debug builds passed and their bundle IDs were checked. In Mac scratch QA, created a workspace, archived its only Genesis 1 tab, saw both recovery actions, hid the sidebar, opened the archive from the empty screen and restored Genesis 1. The archive sheet closed and the sidebar stayed hidden. iPhone UI check remains open: archive the final tab and restore it from the empty screen, then repeat with VoiceOver.

iPhone live follow-up: the reader’s More menu now groups Duplicate, Archive and Close under Tab Actions. In Device Hub on the iOS 27 iPhone 18 Pro Dev simulator, opened Study with one Genesis 1 tab, chose More → Tab Actions → Archive Tab, opened Browse Archived Tabs from the empty state, and tapped Genesis 1. The sheet dismissed and Scripture returned. iOS Dev build passed. VoiceOver and physical-device checks remain open.

Reader-side iPhone archive now shows a dismissible Undo above the dock for the most recent tab archived through More → Tab Actions. It stays scoped to the source workspace and uses the existing restore-and-open route. iOS Dev built and iOS 27 iPhone 18 Pro interaction confirmed that archiving the only Genesis 1 tab showed the notice on the empty screen, and Undo reopened Genesis 1 and removed the notice. Multi-tab reader overlap, landscape, and VoiceOver remain to be tested.

The iPhone Tabs sheet now shows its archive notice for individual swipe/context actions as well as bulk selection. The notice clears any prior move message and announces Undo availability. If the original workspace disappears, the receipt offers Restore Here using the current workspace. iOS Dev built, its bundle ID was checked, and it installed and launched on the iOS 27 simulator. Live checks remain for swipe and context-menu archive, undo after a workspace switch, and the missing-source fallback.

Mac sidebar now offers immediate Undo for Archive Tab and Archive All Tabs, with Restore Here if the source workspace is gone. The notice is shown at the bottom of the sidebar, outside its scrollable tab list. Mac Dev built with its expected bundle ID. Scratch-library QA archived the only Genesis 1 tab through its sidebar context menu, verified the empty state and the Undo notice, and clicked Undo to restore the selected tab and clear the notice. Archive All and the missing-source fallback still need live checks.

iPhone Scripture now includes the visible archive Undo notice in its floating dock clearance. The notice height is measured for larger text, and clearance disappears with the notice or hidden chrome. On the iOS 27 iPhone 18 Pro Dev simulator, duplicated Genesis 1, archived one copy, then selected verse 7 in the remaining tab. Its complete outline and the verse actions appeared above the archive notice and dock. The iOS Dev build passed. Landscape, large accessibility sizes, and the chapter-end case remain open.

iOS 27 iPhone 18 Pro Dev live check on `d9294c2f`: with the archive Undo notice still visible, opened the Dev link for Genesis 1:30–31. The two wrapped verses had one complete dashed outline above the action capsule, archive notice and dock. Tapping Clear removed the outline and actions while verse 31 remained readable. Tapping verse 31 selected it again. Rotated left and returned to portrait, confirming in both orientations the complete final verse stayed above the available actions. In landscape the action capsule sat at bottom leading, opposite the archive notice and dock. This was standard text size. Larger text and the corresponding wrapped Mac chapter-end check remain open.

Mac Dev scratch-library follow-up: opened Genesis 1:30–31 via Go. The wrapped verses appeared in one dashed, faintly filled outline, and the complete final line sat above the floating action capsule. Clear dismissed the capsule, and clicking the verse 31 link selected it again and restored the actions. This covers the normal-width single-chapter reader. Narrow windows and larger reader text remain open.

Verse selection controls expose the selected reference as their accessibility group on iPhone, as on Mac. The compact and overflow action menus carry explicit accessible names on the actual controls, and the narrowest menu now has a Mac tooltip. Both Dev builds passed. Verify with VoiceOver and in a narrow Mac Scripture pane that every action remains accessible through overflow.

## iPhone Scripture note citations

iPhone now uses the same chapter-level citation menu and note cross-reference data as Mac. The chapter menu opens a citing note. An inline arrow is present on verses with note cross references, and opens the inspector on My Notes scoped to that verse. The arrow is excluded from copied Scripture using the existing reader marker attribute. Both Dev builds passed. Live iPhone fixture pending: create a verse note attached to Genesis 1:1 mentioning Exod 20 and Exod 20:11, then open Exodus 20 and check the chapter menu, arrow, scoped reverse reference and marker-free copy.

iPhone live citation verification on `65ce6eff`: seeded one synthetic Genesis 1:1 verse note in the iOS 27 iPhone 18 Pro Dev container. Exodus 20 displayed both the chapter menu and a verse 11 citation arrow. Tapping the arrow opened Cross Refs → My Notes scoped to Exodus 20:11 and showed the Genesis 1:1 backlink. The chapter menu opened the fixture, and its Exodus 20:11 link returned to Scripture. The floating Copy action put Exodus 20:11 text and reference on the simulator clipboard without the arrow. TextKit drag-selection copy and VoiceOver remain open. The fixture remains in the simulator Dev library for repeat checks.

Citation navigation now records the originating tab for chapter-note opens, Cross Refs source notes, and passage opens from the reader, split study panes, or Advanced Search. Both native Dev builds passed. The iOS 27 simulator opened the citing note from Exodus 20, then More → Back returned to Exodus 20:11 with its selection intact. Direct Cross Refs passage and note actions and Mac Back still need live verification for this change.

Mac live Cross Refs follow-up: from selected Genesis 1:1 in the TSKe inspector, chose Proverbs 8:22–24 → Open in New Tab. Back restored the Scripture selection, but the outgoing SwiftUI inspector dismissal had also cleared the source pane. Mac now ignores inspector writebacks for inactive tabs or layouts without an inspector and keeps inspector visibility changes out of passage history. After rebuilding, repeating the route restored Genesis 1:1 with Cross Refs still open. Forward restored Proverbs 8:22–24 with its own inspector still closed. Mac Dev build passed. Closing an inspector manually and the live source-note action remain to check.

iPhone reader More → Notes or Cross References now updates only inspector presentation and tool, preserving the existing tab title and keeping those changes out of passage history. A dismissal reported for an outgoing tab is ignored. The iOS Dev build passed. In the iOS 27 simulator, More → Cross References opened the sheet scoped to Exodus 20:11–12. After dismissal the Dev record retained verses 11–12 and stored the sheet as closed. A tab with a custom title and a cross-tab transition while the sheet is open still need live checks.

Reader display controls now retain the existing Scripture tab title when the passage has not changed. The iPhone Strong’s and Compare toggles update only their own settings, and Compare keeps its existing navigation history and stored format. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA opened Proverbs 8:22–24, enabled Strong’s, and opened and closed the Study Panel. The sidebar still showed the full range label and Back remained disabled throughout. Live iPhone Strong’s and Compare toggles remain to check.

Archive headings now use the saved Scripture passage and range when the group name was generated from its first tab. Stored names and archive records stay intact, and search accepts both the displayed reference and the stored name. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA archived Proverbs 8:22–24, confirmed the range appeared in the sidebar archive and browser heading, then used Undo. Verify iPhone headings and mixed-group partial restores next.

Mac archive browser now exposes Restore Group for multi-tab archives when the list is unfiltered, with actions for the current workspace, original workspace when available, and other live workspaces. Filtered rows still restore individually. Mac Dev built successfully. Scratch QA archived a three-tab workspace, opened the browser, used Restore All 3 Tabs Here, verified the archive cleared and all three tabs returned, and saw the saved active Proverbs tab reopen. Destination submenu, filtered visibility, and partial group recovery remain open for live checks.

Go now distinguishes switching to an exactly matching open Scripture tab from opening a verse destination. It switches without issuing a fresh verse scroll request and records the sending tab for Back. Explicit New Tab and the Mac Change Current Tab shortcut keep their existing routing. Both native Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA used Go from Proverbs to an open Genesis 1:1 tab, confirmed the workspace stayed at three tabs, and used Back to return to Proverbs 8:22–24. After manually scrolling Genesis, repeating Go retained the reading area (scrollbar approximately 48% before and 47% after). iPhone Go interaction and the Mac explicit Change Current Tab path need follow-up.

Mac live follow-up on `446fd828`: with Genesis 1:1 active, ⌘L seeded the field with Genesis 1:1 and typing replaced the selected value. Submitting Proverbs 8:22–24 from that field changed the current row even though the range was already open elsewhere, and the workspace stayed at three tabs. Back restored Genesis 1:1 in the same row. Reopening ⌘L and pressing Escape left that row unchanged. This covers unpinned Scripture-to-Scripture location editing. Pinned and cross-type routing and the iPhone Go flow remain open.

For Scripture matches, Mac ⌘L now says Change Current Tab when the active reader can be edited. From a pinned or otherwise protected reader, an exact open match instead says Switch to Tab and resumes that tab without issuing a fresh verse jump. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA checked both labels, pinned Genesis 1:1, chose the existing Proverbs 8:22–24 tab, and confirmed Back returned to the unmodified pinned Genesis tab without increasing the tab count. The fixture was then unpinned. Cross-type ⌘L and pinned destinations without an exact match remain open for live checks.

Go's exact Scripture results in other workspaces now include the stored workspace name alongside the inferred passage label when those differ. Selection also revalidates the tab's passage, finds an alternate exact tab in the same workspace if available, or reports that the passage is gone. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA searched Genesis 1:1 from the one-tab Workspace 6, chose the result explicitly labeled Workspace 5, and used Back to return to Workspace 6. Both tab counts stayed the same. The stale-result branch, actual cross-workspace scroll offset, and iPhone interaction remain open.

Workspace and tab search now retain the generated workspace name alongside its passage-based label. This also applies to recent tab destinations and to palette workspace rows with a matching passage preview. Both Dev builds passed. In Mac scratch QA a Proverbs search distinguished Workspace 3 (Proverbs 8) from Workspace 5 (Proverbs 8:22–24) in Go and in Find Workspace or Tab, where individual tab rows named their source. iPhone presentation and indistinguishable copies within one workspace remain open.

Workspace tab search now numbers tabs that share the same visible title and detail within one workspace. Results also identify pinned and current tabs. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA searched Proverbs from Workspace 6, saw two Proverbs 8:22–24 rows in Workspace 5 with distinct Tab 1 and Tab 2 labels, and verified Tab 2 opened the second sidebar row. iPhone presentation and pinned/current markers remain to verify live.

iPhone Tabs now shows the same-title tab numbers in both list and grid while a search is active, including matches from the current workspace. It uses the same numbering as workspace search, based on pinned-first workspace order and computed before applying the query or recent sort. Both Dev builds and git diff --check passed. Live simulator checks remain for grid/list, a pinned pair, and a query that hides one of the two tabs.

Mac sidebar filtering now uses the same duplicate-title numbering as workspace search. The label is visible and included in tooltip and accessibility text during filtering. Mac Dev built, and scratch QA confirmed filtering for Proverbs displayed Tab 1 and Tab 2 with the second row selected. Escape removed the numbers and preserved selection. Grouped-by-space and type-only filtering need live checks.

Mac sidebar search and type filter now retain their criteria independently for each workspace during the window session. Scratch QA filtered Workspace 5 by Proverbs, switched to Workspace 6 and back, and confirmed the search and selected Tab 2 were restored with focus on the reader window. Type-only filters and app relaunch remain outside this live check. Filter memory is window-local.

iPhone Tabs keeps the workspace switcher available during search and type filtering. iPhone Tabs filter menu now groups Space, Tab Type, and Sort in named submenus, showing active filter values at the first level. iPhone Tabs offers Show All Tabs when search or filters yield no matches. Mac sidebar now offers Show All Tabs when a query or type filter leaves no rows. It closes the filter using the same reset path as Escape. Mac Dev built successfully. Notes Insert now exposes Heading levels and Bullet List from the original mobile insert workflow, plus direct Table insertion. It calls the existing rich-editor commands on both platforms. Notes Insert → Link now prompts for a web URL and inserts a Markdown link. iPhone Notes now sends the keyboard accessory and selection Format → Insert Link commands to the same URL prompt as the Insert menu. The keyboard and selection routes pass the original TextKit range through the prompt so focus changes do not change the intended label. Both native Dev builds passed. iOS 27 iPhone 18 Pro simulator live follow-up on `50e140c4`: in the Dev citation fixture, focused the note body and opened Insert Link from the keyboard accessory overflow. Cancel returned to the original caret without editing. iPhone Notes selection Format → Highlight now exposes the same six colors as the main Format menu and an explicit Remove Highlight action. Mac and iPhone Notes Format → Highlight now includes Remove Highlight in the editor toolbar, using the same default and named-mark removal as the iPhone selection menu. Both Dev builds passed. ### 2026-11-17 Notes typography and tables

Mac and iPhone now draw editable Markdown tables with a shaded header, cell dividers, and the same font as the rest of the note. Incomplete tables and lines outside a recognized table keep their ordinary text layout. Notes previews also show vertical dividers. Inline Code is available from Format on both platforms and from the iPhone selection menu, and explicit code keeps its monospaced font.

Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA pasted a table with prose and inline code, checked its grid visually, and edited a populated cell. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator verified Insert → Table with the corrected literal separator plus Undo and Redo. The focused AppKit checks measured aligned columns with Unicode content and confirmed the Markdown source stayed intact. The focused inline-code checks and Mac format toggle passed. The full SwiftPM run was blocked by local package source and resource staging timeouts.

UIKit had replaced the separator's ASCII hyphens with em dashes in new iPhone tables. The insertion path now preserves the literal delimiter. Next check existing affected Dev notes and recover the separator only where there is clear table structure, then verify populated cells, large Dynamic Type, and narrow and wide tables on iPhone. Keep the correction display-only where possible and retain the original note bytes until an explicit edit.

Follow-up: the rich editors and note previews recognize the older iPhone em-dash separator between matching rows. The parser leaves em-dash cells inside a valid table and standalone rows alone. It preserves the original Markdown bytes. Focused Swift checks covered those cases. On the iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, reopening the exact fixture inserted by the old build displayed the grid. Next verify populated cells with large Dynamic Type, wide tables, and the preview's rendering of the recovered fixture.

iPhone Notes now restyles its attributed body and recalculates table geometry when Dynamic Type changes. The iOS Dev build passed. On the iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, entered a header into the recovered table, changed Dynamic Type from Large to Accessibility Large with the caret in the cell, and verified that the text and grid grew immediately. Restoring Large shrank both without leaving the editor. Wide tables with multiple populated columns still need a separate check.

Wide tables now outline each logical row when cell text wraps, retaining the shaded header and visible field breaks. Trailing separator padding no longer creates an empty line in otherwise short rows. Mac scratch QA showed a populated three-column table wrapping with visually separate rows. A focused AppKit layout check then confirmed the final padding change kept short rows single-line, wrapped the long row, and preserved source. Both final Dev builds passed. The populated wide fixture on iPhone remains unverified. In compact layout, wrapped rows still flow across the available width and do not preserve vertical cell alignment on continuation lines.

Mac controls using the shared clickable cursor now read their effective SwiftUI enabled state, including disabled ancestor views such as the reader beneath floating search. The native toolbar cursor observer now stores enabled states against weak view keys, so closed and rebuilt toolbars release their tracking state. Mac Dev built successfully. Direct visual cursor checks around the verse capsule and during native Back/Forward enablement still need a moving-pointer check. The restored scripture overlay, grouped dashed runs, and Mac resize reveal fix remain in place.

Migrated the remaining direct pointing-hand styles in the Mac chapter toolbar, sidebar rows, history controls and Notes preview links to the shared enabled-state modifier. Floating search gives its dismissible backdrop a standard cursor. Both Dev builds passed. In Mac scratch QA, Genesis 1 disabled Previous Chapter, Next opened Genesis 2, both chapter buttons were disabled under Command-K, and Escape re-enabled them. The accessibility tree verified control states and navigation. Cursor glyph rendering during physical pointer movement remains unverified.

Rechecked I25 on iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, iOS 27, build `748d3feb`. Opened John 3:2 through a Dev link and verified the five action icons over red-letter verse 8 in both appearances. In dark appearance, tapping the bodies of verses 3 and 4 produced one dashed outline for John 3:2–4. Restored light appearance, then tapped Clear and verified the outline and action bar disappeared. No capsule styling change was needed for this reproduction. Physical iPhone remains open.

Mac and iPhone Archive Inactive Tabs now offer Review Selection. It shows every eligible checked tab, including tabs hidden by the current query or type filter. Uncheck individual tabs there and use Back to Matches to restore the filter. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA selected Genesis 2, typed a query that hid it, confirmed Review showed the checked tab, then returned to the original query and canceled with the workspace count unchanged. iPhone interaction and review of an actual batch archive or move remain unverified.

iPhone 18 Pro Simulator verified the cleanup review with 17 tabs. Selected John 3:17, applied the Notes type filter, confirmed the hidden count and reviewed the still-checked Scripture tab. Back to Matches restored the type filter. The cleanup sheet now titles its review and matches states and returns to matching tabs after archive or move. Verified both titles and Archive → Undo in the final iPhone build. The scratch workspace returned to 17 tabs with its prior archive group count. Both Dev builds passed. Moving from review and larger batches remain unverified.

Workspace pickers now show a compact “1 of 2” label when displayed names collide, including inferred passage names and case or accent variants. The Mac workspace menu, workspace search, iPhone workspace list, and tab move destination labels use it. The move list numbers live workspaces before excluding the current one. This is display-only and does not affect matching legacy names, selected destination IDs, or persisted names. Focused Swift checks covered identical generated previews, equal names with different case, unique names and unchanged IDs. Next verify duplicate labels and destination selection live on both platforms, especially with long workspace lists.

Mac scratch QA created two “QA Mirror” workspaces with the same Genesis 1 preview. The switcher showed 1 of 2 and 2 of 2, and selecting each changed the active indicator to the matching ordinal. Rename now shows the stored name on both platforms. On Mac, a generated-name workspace displayed a Genesis 1 preview but the Rename field showed Workspace 4. Canceled and returned to Study. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone duplicate-name switching and move destination labels remain open.

Workspace disambiguation now follows tab search results, recent tabs and already-open passage destinations on both platforms. These routes share the live workspace labels used by Move Tab. Mac scratch QA searched QA Mirror Genesis 1, verified that the two identical tab titles showed 1 of 2 and 2 of 2, then opened the second result and observed the matching active workspace. Returned the scratch app to Study. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone results, recent entries, and the open-passage reuse list still need direct interaction checks.

Archived group titles and restore-to-source actions now show the duplicate-aware live workspace label. Historical names are still searchable and remain the fallback when the source is unavailable. Mac scratch QA archived Genesis 1 from QA Mirror 2 of 2, viewed it from Study, and verified the archived title and Restore to QA Mirror · 2 of 2. Restoring put Genesis back into the second workspace without switching away from Study. Both Dev builds passed. The same archive labels need a live iPhone check.

iPhone Workspaces now shows the current workspace first in the unfiltered list and keeps New Workspace in the header. Search results retain their prior ordering, and the stored workspace order is untouched. The iOS Dev build passed. On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, verified Study Phone and Study each became the first checked row after switching. The header plus created Workspace 5 and opened its one-tab reader. Reopening showed the matching Genesis 1 preview with its generated name and ordinal. Returned to Study Phone. Search with duplicate names and move destination routing on iPhone remain open.

Workspace search on both platforms now opens a unique exact workspace or a single tab match on Return. Multiple exact workspaces, multiple tab matches, and multiple workspace-only matches keep the search open with a prompt to choose. Mac scratch QA searched QA Mirror Genesis 1 and verified Return left both numbered matches visible. Changing the query to QA Mirror Jude 1 and pressing Return opened the single result in QA Mirror 2 of 2. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone keyboard submission still needs a live check.

iPhone workspace rows now show stored name, tab count and ordinal immediately below the title, before the passage previews. Refocusing search, entering Workspace 5 and pressing Return opened that exact workspace. Returned to Study Phone by tapping the blank trailing part of its workspace row. Advanced Scripture Search now offers Open Verse, Highlight and Remove Highlights through the row menu and overflow on both platforms. Highlight is stored against the result’s edition and uses the reader’s whole-verse color semantics. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA searched Nicodemus, applied Yellow to John 3:4 from results, opened the reader to verify the mark, then removed it from search and verified that Open Verse returned to the same unhighlighted verse. Advanced Scripture Search rows now identify saved highlights separately from yellow search-term matching. The indicator uses the whole-verse color when present and is neutral for partial marks. Each lazy row reloads its result edition’s chapter marks when the highlight revision changes and disables Remove Highlights when the verse has none. Both Dev builds passed. In Mac scratch QA, applying Yellow to John 3:4 added the Highlighted accessibility label and enabled removal. Removing it cleared the label and disabled removal without leaving results. iPhone rendering and performance with very long result lists remain unverified.

Search result rows share a per-view highlight cache keyed by book, chapter and text edition. Empty chapters are cached too; load failures remain retryable. Changes to saved highlights invalidate the cache on the next lookup, so visible rows update without repeated reads from the same chapter. A focused Swift check counted one load across 1,000 lookups in the same chapter and fresh loads for a different edition and a newer revision. Both Dev builds passed. Device timing and the iPhone live search menu still need verification.

Advanced Scripture Search now marks the currently saved whole-verse color with a checkmark. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator searched Nicodemus, applied Blue to John 3:4 via overflow, saw the separate blue indicator, opened the menu by long press, and removed the mark without leaving search. The final build showed the Blue checkmark in the submenu and removed the test mark again. Long-list device timing and physical iPhone QA remain open.

On iPhone, Scripture Search Options now stacks each picker caption above its selected value, including Order, Group by and Match Tags. The menu choices and accessibility names remain in the pickers. Both Dev builds passed. The iPhone 18 Pro Simulator showed the labeled controls at standard size and Accessibility Large. Scrolled to the bottom and verified that Order and all three Context choices were reachable and readable. Restored the original text size. VoiceOver and the Books keyboard interaction remain unverified.

Scripture Search Options now offers Browse Books on both platforms. It expands the installed book catalog, deduplicates across editions, and filters by full or short name as you type. Selected books remain removable chips. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator opened the list without a query, typed John and saw John plus 1–3 John, then selected John. The Nicodemus search narrowed from seven results to five. Clearing the chip restored seven. Live Mac and iPhone software keyboard layout checks remain open.

Browse Books and typed book suggestions now honor the selected text editions and testament. Existing book choices stay visible as removable chips when the search scope changes. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator verified New Testament browsing starts at Matthew, a Genesis query returns No matching books, and Reset Options makes Genesis available again. Restored the original search. Edition-specific live catalog coverage remains open.

Scripture Search now explains when selected books and the active edition or testament have no overlap. Clear Book Filter removes only the book restriction. Other empty filtered searches identify the active filters as part of the search. Mac scratch QA applied John and Old Testament to the Nicodemus query, saw the mismatch explanation, and cleared only Books while Old Testament stayed active. Clearing Testament restored the seven results. iPhone live empty-state interaction remains open.

Lexicon occurrences now offer Reset Filters next to the empty filtered state in the full entry and compact inspector. The same reset action clears edition, book, translated words, sort, page limit and filter-panel query. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA combined goddess and 1 Samuel on H430, closed the filter panel, and verified Reset Filters restored the Genesis occurrence rows without reopening the panel. The iPhone compact inspector and its loaded-result limit remain to verify live.

Compact Lexicon puts Open Full Lexicon beside Filters and Search Scripture. Truncated occurrence sets are described as a sample, and their counts distinguish matches within that sample. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone Simulator opened the inline H7225 compact entry from Genesis 1 and showed its complete 49-passage count. A related H7218 entry opened in the same panel, but the UI bridge did not reach its occurrence section, so the truncated wording and full-entry handoff remain unverified in the running app.

Opening Full Lexicon from a filtered compact entry transfers its edition, book, translated words and sort into the destination tab. A changed transfer resets the destination scroll and starts at the first page. Opening from an unfiltered preview preserves any existing destination filters. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA opened H430 from Genesis 1, verified the 60-passage preview, selected 1 Chronicles, and opened the full entry with that filter intact. Full showed 101 of 2,242 passages and clearing the book restored 2,242. Live iPhone transition and the other filter types remain open.

Lexicon paging controls now wrap at narrow widths. Show Fewer appears after loading any additional page, even when more occurrences remain. Show More clamps the final page to the actual count. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA filtered H430 to the 101 passages in 1 Chronicles, used Show More, confirmed Show More, Show All and Show Fewer were all available, and collapsed back to the first page. Search Scripture then opened H430 search and Command-[ returned to the same filtered Lexicon entry. Cleared the scratch filter. Live iPhone paging and history remain open.

Show Fewer in Lexicon now returns the viewport to the Occurrences heading and first page. An explicit Back-to-occurrence request remains pending until the destination view is active and its scroll can be issued. Both Dev builds passed. In Mac scratch QA, expanded H430, collapsed it and saw the heading at the top. Opened Genesis 1:5 from the occurrence list and Command-[ returned to H430 with Genesis 1:5 near the center. ### Notes typography and editable table layout (2026-11-17)

The Mac editor uses the standard macOS body size. Both editors align the body with its title and preserve the saved Notes Font setting. Inline and fenced code keep their monospace styling. The shared TextKit table layout collapses source padding only for display, applies Markdown column alignment, and gives rows consistent insets and clearer dividers. When earlier columns leave room, continuation lines in a long final cell stay in that column and the grid retains its vertical dividers. For very narrow or heavily populated tables, the existing wrapped-row fallback remains.

Both native Dev builds passed. The Mac scratch library showed a long final cell wrapping within the last column of a three-column note with a Scripture link. The iPhone 18 Pro Simulator displayed the shaded header and dividers after Insert → Table. Next check populated narrow tables on iPhone, tables with long cells before the final column, right/center alignment with mixed inline styles, and display at accessibility text sizes. The production library was outside this check.

### Notes table previews and printing (2026-11-17)

Table previews share the editor's pipe parser, preserve separate adjacent tables, and draw vertical dividers through the full height of multiline rows. Printed tables use the source's column alignment and equal printable column widths. Mac measures the full typeset document before opening the print panel. This corrected a three-column scratch fixture whose print preview showed cramped first columns and clipped the remaining rows. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA confirmed all three columns and the final row were visible in the print panel, then canceled. Version-history table preview, populated iPhone print preview, and multi-page print pagination still need live checks. Supporting cell-local wrapping before the last column in the live editor remains open.

### Notes table boundaries and literal pipes (2026-11-17)

Editable table commands now use confirmed header, separator, and row widths to identify the table at the caret. Adjacent tables remain independent even without a blank line between them. Within a recognized table, escaped pipes display literally in prose and inline code while the TextKit source keeps the escape syntax. Unrelated lines retain their existing display. Both Dev builds and focused Swift checks passed. Mac scratch QA observed a literal pipe in the first table, added a column to the second table without changing the first, and used Undo. iPhone edit actions and cell-local wrapping before the last column still need work. Short Markdown rows with omitted trailing cells remain eligible for the existing table commands.

### Cross Refs chapter navigation (2026-11-17)

Cross Refs now offers Expand All and Collapse All above the verse groups when more than one verse has results. Whole-row single-verse toggles and the source menu remain available. Expanded verse numbers survive a source refresh or Notes revision in the same scope, and reset on passage or scope change. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA switched Genesis 1:5 to whole-chapter TSKe and verified expansion and collapse. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator repeated the interaction in the John 3 sheet. Classic, My Notes refresh, very long chapters, and keyboard/VoiceOver activation remain to verify.

### iPhone Scripture chapter-end clearance (2026-11-17)

Changing the floating controls' bottom inset now keeps a reader already at the chapter end anchored to that end. It uses the same eight-point tolerance as the dock's end detection. At earlier positions, the reader retains its offset. Both Dev builds passed. On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, opened Obadiah 1:21, cleared and reselected the wrapped final verse, then added verse 20. The complete final verse and single grouped outline stayed above both floating controls. The scrub from hidden to visible dock during a continuous drag, landscape, and physical-device checks remain open.

### Mac Scripture chapter-end clearance and Notes Unicode (2026-11-17)

Mac now keeps the chapter end anchored when selection adds or removes the floating action bar's scroll inset. It uses the same AppKit end calculation when restyling the chapter. Live scratch QA opened Obadiah 1:21 with Cross Refs narrowing the reader, cleared selection, and clicked verse 21 again. Every wrapped line remained above the floating action bar. Wider layouts and pointer-scroll transitions remain open.

The shared Notes plain-text formatter now appends contiguous UTF-16 ranges so emoji and other supplementary characters survive previews and plain-text copy. The previous escaped-pipe regression exposed this under Swift Testing. It now passes alongside all 147 Swift Testing cases in 44 suites, and both Dev builds passed.

### Find repeated tabs in a workspace (2026-11-17)

Mac sidebar and iPhone Tabs filters now offer Repeated Titles. It shows tabs in the current workspace whose displayed title and details match another tab, without requiring a search term. Combine it with existing type filters and remove it via the active filter control. On iPhone, compact list and grid rows also number matching copies during ordinary browsing. Mac retains the filter when switching away from and back to a workspace in the same run. This is a browsing filter, independent of Exact Duplicates cleanup, which compares saved tab state. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA duplicated Obadiah 1:21, saw only Tab 1 and Tab 2 when filtered, and cleared the filter to restore the other tabs. iPhone touch, list reordering with numbered copies, and combined filters remain open.

### Workspace picker content hints (2026-11-17)

When workspace labels collide, picker previews now prefer an open tab title that appears in only one of those workspaces. Matching uses case- and diacritic-insensitive labels and counts each tab title once per workspace. Generated workspace aliases, stored names, and numbered disambiguation are preserved. Explicit search still chooses previews that match the query. Both Dev builds and focused Swift checks passed, including two generated Session 8 entries with identical first two tabs and different later tabs. Mac scratch QA opened the QA Mirror entry identified by Jude 1 and confirmed that workspace contained the Jude tab. iPhone layout, long menu labels, and very large workspace lists remain open.

### Browse another iPhone workspace's tabs (2026-11-17)

In iPhone Tabs → Workspaces, long-press a live workspace or reveal its leading swipe action and choose Browse Tabs. The page lists and searches that workspace's open tabs in pinned-first display order while the current reader stays in place. Opening a row uses the existing workspace/tab navigation path, including the return history. Same-title numbers and pinned labels are visible in the list. Both Dev builds passed. On iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, long-pressed Study from Study Phone, saw John 3 and Untitled while Obadiah stayed in the reader, then opened the existing John 3 tab. Leading swipe, search, and Back after cross-workspace open still need direct checks. Saved snapshots continue to open through the existing workspace action.

### Browse another Mac workspace's tabs (2026-11-17)

Mac Workspace → Find Workspace or Tab now has a Browse Tabs button beside each live workspace. It opens the shared workspace tab list used by iPhone, with scoped search, pinned order, and numbered copies. Opening a result switches directly to the existing tab. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA browsed Study from QA Mirror, typed Obadiah, saw two numbered matches, opened the second, and used Command-[ to return to Jude 1 in QA Mirror. Physical iPhone, Mac keyboard traversal, and VoiceOver activation after the shared-view refactor remain to check.

### Keyboard search within workspace tabs (2026-11-17)

Mac's workspace tab browser focuses search on opening. On both platforms, a query ending in Tab N filters by the displayed copy number, and submitting a nonempty query only opens a tab when exactly one result remains. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA confirmed autofocus, the two matches for Obadiah, and Return opening Study's Obadiah Tab 2. Return with multiple matches leaves the list open but Mac can move focus off the field. iPhone keyboard submission and Mac VoiceOver remain to check.

### Scripture Search scroll across repeated history (2026-11-17)

Word and tag-only search now restore through a tracked ScrollPosition. The clicked result stays centered across repeated Back/Forward visits. A later manual scroll or Jump to section records the updated position for ordinary tab switches. Mac scratch QA scrolled in H430's 2,242 matches, opened Genesis 3:3, and saw two Back visits return to the same scroll-bar value, with Forward reopening Genesis 3:3. A subsequent manual scroll followed by a Lexicon tab switch restored the later viewport within one row. iPhone touch and tagged-only live verification remain open.

### Notes search and context-menu history (2026-11-17)

Notes search keeps an opened result as its centering anchor across repeated Back and Forward. A deliberate scroll replaces that anchor with the regular offset. The row button and context-menu Open and Open in New Tab all record the originating Notes list and matching result. Both native builds passed. Mac scratch QA searched Neighbor, opened the sole note from its context menu in a new tab, used Back to recover the query and result, then repeated Forward and Back. Centering a result in a long Notes list and the iPhone context menu remain to verify. The iPhone long-press preview still uses raw Markdown; use the version-preview renderer for that surface in a separate change.

### Formatted iPhone Notes previews (2026-11-17)

Long-pressing a note now renders its formatted excerpt with the shared Notes preview renderer. The preview is bounded to the first 4,000 characters and keeps the native context actions. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator displayed a shaded table header and grid in a long-press preview, then opened the same note in the editor. Longer excerpts, callouts and accessibility sizes remain to verify. The compact list row still shows raw pipe delimiters for a table-only note and should be cleaned separately.

### Readable Notes table excerpts (2026-11-17)

Notes list, note search, and Go now derive excerpts from the same recognized tables used in Notes previews. They omit Markdown borders and alignment rows and show nonempty cell contents separated by a middle dot. Saved Markdown and copy/export flows are outside this rendering change. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA saw the table header as readable text in the Notes list and found a later table cell via search. A literal escaped pipe remained visible. iPhone list layout, Go, and long-note performance remain to check.

### Direct source notes from Cross Refs (2026-11-17)

My Notes cross-reference rows now make the citing note title a direct button beneath its excerpt on both platforms. On iPhone, the Scripture study inspector closes before a Cross Refs destination opens, avoiding a blank sheet over the destination. Both Dev builds passed. iPhone 18 Pro Simulator opened the synthetic source note from the Exodus 20:11 citation, confirmed the sheet closed, and used dock Back to recover Exodus 20:11 with the selection intact. Mac source-note activation, wider inspectors, and opening another passage through the iPhone inspector remain to check.

### Lexicon occurrence filter feedback (2026-11-17)

Book choices follow Scripture order. Book and rendering counts are aligned separately from their names. Both scoped option searches show an explicit empty state and have a clear-query button. Clear Selected Words resets only the rendering selections without closing the picker. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA opened H430, confirmed Genesis and Exodus order and counts, searched for an absent book, cleared the query, and selected and cleared gods. ### Compare exit and retained passage (2026-11-17)

Close Column names its transition to the reader when the comparison would have one or zero columns left. A legacy comparison with one saved column goes to that column's passage when closed. On iPhone, the explicit Exit Compare button saves the visible verse from the main translation when available, keeping the comparison configuration for reentry. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA closed KJVA from a Genesis 2 comparison, confirmed the single LXX reader and Compare off, then used Back to restore both columns. iPhone Simulator exited Compare for selected Genesis 3:3 and recovered the selected reader. Legacy single-column state and exit after manually scrolling the comparison remain to verify.

### Compare reading positions across layouts

Stacked comparisons now save each visible column's verse after scrolling. Reordering carries those bookmarks with the columns, and returning to one reader can use a saved or legacy column verse. Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA opened an independently scrolled Genesis 2 LXX column in the reader near its end, then used Back to recover the two panes. Live stacked transitions and drag reordering remain open.

### Mac reader Find, sidebar double-click, and location editing

Double-clicking the blank Mac sidebar tab area opens the New Tab command palette. Single drags still route to AppKit window dragging, and the separate header retains its existing behavior. Command-L prefers the active matching tab type over automatic reuse, including pinned Scripture tabs, and keeps the existing selected location text. Ordinary Go still uses the normal reuse rules.

Mac Scripture Find now floats in glass below the toolbar without resizing the reader. Typing outside an editor starts a search and hides the bar after six idle seconds. Command-F opens a persistent focused search, Command-G and Shift-Command-G step matches, and Escape closes it. The active match is highlighted in the reader and the search excludes generated Strong's and verse markers. The AppKit reader cursor checks the full window hit view and excludes the native title bar before setting its text cursor.

Both Dev builds passed. Mac scratch QA verified New Tab from a blank double-click, Command-L replacing the current Scripture tab when another tab already showed the destination, and replacement in a pinned tab with the tab count unchanged. Also verified type-ahead and idle dismissal, Command-F focus, Command-G, persistent match highlighting, and Escape. Pointer movement and flicker over every toolbar button, IME input, and compact reader widths remain to verify. The iPhone reader still uses native Find.

### Keep Mac Find active when Scripture display changes

A same-passage TextKit rebuild now recomputes the current Find results and reapplies the active highlight without scrolling the viewport again. The existing match index is retained when possible and clamped when the result count changes. Both Dev builds passed. In Mac scratch QA, Find for grace in Romans 5 stayed on match 2 of 6 while enabling Strong's numbers. Command-G advanced to 3 of 6, and Escape closed the bar. Font and width changes and IME input remain open for live QA.

### Mac Find in narrow reader panes

### 2026-11-18 — iPhone verse taps and native word selection coexist
Supersedes the temporary trace/prototype checkpoint. Production source has NO trace/helper/env-key references. Final clean builds PASS: /tmp/berean-reader-selection-final-{mac,ios}.log, followed by iOS-only verse-marker refinement /tmp/berean-reader-number-control-final-ios.log. Normal simulator app installed without tracing. Preview preparation commit736dd36d is included in these builds; its final variable rename is now compiled.

Root cause: rapid taps in different verses can trigger UIKit word double-tap although UITouch.tapCount is1 for each touch. Cache touch-down intent (layout can move before recognition); use documented dynamic gesture failure priority for different verses within0...0.5seconds. Same-verse body double-tap remains native. Verse-number taps are controls and take priority over other tap recognizers, preserving their accessibility action and all non-verse links. Meaningful native selection triggers a deferred weak/passage-guarded callback to clear whole-verse mode while retaining native handles/menu. No private recognizer names used as production conditions, no UIKit delegate replacement or disabled native selection. Apple primary reference: https://developer.apple.com/documentation/uikit/preferring-one-gesture-over-another .

Diagnosis evidence (temporary /tmp files only): /tmp/berean-reader-tap-trace-runtime.log original conflict; /tmp/berean-reader-cross-verse-priority-runtime.log failed tapCount gate; /tmp/berean-reader-cross-verse-time-runtime.log timestamp fix; /tmp/berean-reader-exclusive-selection-runtime.log native mode handoff. ## 2026-11-19 — Source-preserving table formatting and compact note actions

## 2026-11-19 — Shared multi-cell/multiline note highlights (8adef50b)

Both native note adapters now use BereanNotes.NoteInlineFormat.highlightEdit for Default/color/Remove Highlight. The old line-only wrapper and separate removal regex are replaced by one source edit. Confirmed table cells and each paragraph receive their own marks; table pipes, alignment rules, line endings, heading/list/quote prefixes and code/link/other inline constructs are preserved. Existing code/links/other marks remain protected, not newly combined with the chosen color; arbitrary nested rich marks remain open. Applying a color is idempotent, mixed colors recolor without nested wrappers, default yellow toggles across eligible text, and explicit Remove handles many cells/paragraphs at once. Partial recolor/removal splits existing runs, retains outside colors/visible words and maps the selected body offsets. Removing at a caret inside a mark clears that run; caret attempts inside code/links/other marks do nothing. Invalid ranges and malformed color identifiers are rejected. No saved-note migration, automatic repair or schema/sync changes.

An escaped-pipe regression initially failed the readable-text invariant: treating its hidden backslash as unrelated markup split it from the pipe and could create a column. Fixed by keeping the escape atom together, including partial boundary selections. The same boundary correction applies to ordinary inline table formatting. ## 2026-11-19 — Combined note styles, bounded parsing and visible-only backgrounds

**09eb7d84** uses Foundation Markdown source-position attributes for strong/emphasis ranges in the original UTF-16 storage. It handles shared/partial delimiter boundaries (`***word***`, `***word** rest*`, `**rest *word***`), while Berean keeps underline/highlight/wiki/image syntax and raw link destinations. Extended outer marks may enclose parsed code/links/other styles without reusing their delimiters; literal code remains literal. A legacy fallback preserves the native writer’s punctuation-only marks such as `A**\|**B`, which strict CommonMark leaves literal. No serialization or saved content is changed by rendering. Design source: [Apple Markdown source positions](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/foundation/attributescopes/foundationattributes/markdownsourceposition). Installed SDK interfaces and `/tmp/berean-markdown-source-position-probe.swift` confirmed exact source ranges for emoji/combining/Hebrew, code and links.

Parsing-only release benchmark, 100-line mixed note parsed100times: old0.157235s; initial uncached implementation0.719633s; bounded cached implementation0.156760s (160000spans versus old150000). These are local synthetic parser measurements, not whole-editor typing latency. Cache is process-only, lock-protected, at most512bodies/65536UTF16 units, bypasses individual bodies>4096units and reapplies local offsets for each block. No disk/settings/sync state. Concurrent-note and body-reuse/offset tests pass. Benchmark sources/executables under `/tmp/berean-note-combined-benchmark-{old,new}`; final `/tmp/berean-note-combined-benchmark-cached.log`.

The initial6 new renderer tests failed31 assertions, demonstrating the gap. The first implementation then failed a partial escaped-pipe compatibility invariant; preserving the native punctuation-mark fallback fixed it. Another test fixture put an unescaped wiki-alias pipe into a GFM table, making its body column count invalid; corrected that fixture by testing the alias in a separate paragraph. **Do not count that invalid fixture as an app regression.** Separately queue actual legacy GFM escaped-alias metadata normalization: serializer escapes table-cell pipes and the native wiki regex may retain the escape in the target. Verify actual exported fixtures and literal-backslash titles before changing identity resolution.

Partial highlight recolor/removal through nested code/link/other style syntax could insert tags inside literal markup. Guarded those edits: a complete visible highlighted run includes its hidden delimiters and can be cleared safely; unsupported partial protected runs remain unchanged. This is not arbitrary nested rich-mark editing parity. Ordinary partial plain-highlight edits remain supported.9new combined-style/cache/safety tests; final46 focused tests/5suites PASS `/tmp/berean-note-combined-tests.log`. Full actual-repo package suite PASS **232 tests** (4core +228conformance/62suites), `/tmp/berean-note-combined-full-suite.log`,110.292s. No golden JSON changed. ## 2026-11-19 — Semantic combined-style edits in both native Notes editors

NoteInlineFormat now edits a visible styled selection using actual semantic coverage. Code/metadata remain protected. Partial removal from a larger mark still returns nil; whole-cell/source selections containing hidden syntax use the existing path and do not establish arbitrary nested editing parity.

## 2026-11-19 — Partial style removal with global semantic validation

NoteInlineFormat splits an enclosing style around a visible selection, preserving surrounding words and their formatting. Foundation divides an outer emphasis into runs around nested emphasis/link/code changes: join fragments only across hidden syntax before resolving outer delimiters. Exact whole-run shared boundaries keep the previous direct removal behavior. Split segments keep whitespace outside their new marks. Both asterisk/underscore source forms work, and emitted wrappers use the requested valid style delimiter.

Validation now compares unhidden UTF16 character sequences and semantic coverage by visible position across the entire note, rather than only the selected word. Preserve every other style, named highlight color, code span, link destination, wiki target and image annotation; remove/add the requested style only on selected non-whitespace characters. Whitespace-only style coverage differences created by trimming split delimiters are intentionally ignored. Existing rendered plain-text equality and local requested-style checks remain. Unsupported candidates return nil instead of altering their metadata. This still does not establish arbitrary mixed/whole-source/whole-table mark mutation parity.

Initial plain splitting passed16tests. The stronger validator initially rejected splitting because surrounding spaces lost invisible mark coverage; ignore whitespace for style coverage. Two nested cases then exposed fragmented Foundation runs without adjacent outer delimiters; joining across hidden syntax fixed them. Final18focused tests pass `/tmp/berean-note-partial-style-tests.log`. Earlier interim full/build logs are superseded by final **240tests** (4core+236conformance/62suites),176.494s, `/tmp/berean-note-partial-style-final-full-suite.log`; final Mac/iOS builds PASS `/tmp/berean-note-partial-style-final-{mac,ios}.log`.
